\hskip-2.15277pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{(2-L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}=\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{\eta_{t}(2-L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(48)

Now, let us denote
g_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n}\widetilde{\nabla}{\Phi}_{\gamma}(w_{i}^{(t)})
.
Then, from the update of
w_{i}^{(t)}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}g_{t}=\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-w_{n}^{(t)})=\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(w_{0}^{(t)}-w_{n}^{(t)}).\end{array}

Since
\widetilde{w}_{t}=\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}(w_{n}^{(t)})
, we have
\nabla{f}(\widetilde{w}_{t}):=\eta_{t}^{-1}\big(w_{n}^{(t)}-\widetilde{w}_{t}\big)=-g_{t}-\eta_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\in\partial{f}(\widetilde{w}_{t})
.
Hence, by the convexity of
f
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}f(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&f(\widehat{w}^{t})+\langle\nabla{f}(\widetilde{w}_{t}),\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle=f(\widehat{w}^{t})-\langle g_{t},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle-\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}\langle\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle\\
&=&f(\widehat{w}^{t})-\langle g_{t},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle+\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\big[\|\widehat{w}^{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

Again, by the
L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}
-smoothness of
\Phi_{\gamma}
from (
34
) of Lemma
4
, we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Phi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Phi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\langle\nabla{\Phi}_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}),\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\rangle+\frac{L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}}{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2},\\
\Phi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})&\leq&\Phi_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}^{t})+\langle\nabla{\Phi}_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}),\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle+\frac{L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}}{2}\|\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Adding the last three inequalities together, and using
\Psi_{\gamma}(w)=f(w)+\Phi_{\gamma}(w)
and
\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}=-\eta_{t}{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}^{t})+\langle\nabla{\Phi}_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-g_{t},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{(1+L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\Psi_{\gamma}(\widehat{w}^{t})+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|\nabla{\Phi}_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-g_{t}\|^{2}-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta_{t}(1+L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2},\end{array}

(49)

where we have used Young’s inequality in the last line as
\langle\nabla{\Phi}_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-g_{t},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle\leq\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|\nabla{\Phi}_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-g_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\|^{2}
.

Summing up (
48
) and (
49
), we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|\nabla{\Phi}_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-g_{t}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(50)

Using (
39
) with
g_{t}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n}\widetilde{\nabla}{\Phi}_{\gamma}(w_{i}^{(t)})
, we arrive at

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\cdot\mathcal{T}_{[n]}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:grad_Phi_bound2}}{\leq}&\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\frac{1}{n}\left(C_{1}+2C_{2}L_{F}^{2}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|w_{j-1}^{(t)}-w^{(t)}_{0}\|^{2},\end{array}

which is (
47
), where
L_{\Psi_{\gamma}}:=C_{1}+2C_{2}L_{F}^{2}=\frac{2M_{F}^{4}\|K\|^{4}}{(\mu_{h}+\gamma)^{2}}+4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}L_{F}^{2}
.
∎

B.3
The proof of Theorem
6
and Corollary
1
for Algorithm
1

Let us recall that
C_{1}:=\frac{2M_{F}^{4}\|K\|^{4}}{(\mu_{h}+\gamma)^{2}}
,
C_{2}:=2M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}
, and
L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}:=M_{h}\|K\|L_{F}+\frac{M_{F}^{2}\|K\|^{2}}{\mu_{h}+\gamma}
from Lemma
4
.
To prove Theorem
6
, we will need the following lemma.

Lemma 10
.

Let
\{w_{i}^{(t)}\}
be generated by Algorithm
1
using arbitrarily permutations
\pi^{(t)}=\hat{\pi}^{(t)}
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta>0
such that
\left(2C_{1}+4C_{2}L_{F}^{2}\right)\eta^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
4L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta+8L_{\Psi}\Lambda_{0}\eta^{2}\leq 1
.
Then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{4}{T\eta}\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}^{0})-\Psi^{\star}_{\gamma}\right]+8L_{\Psi}(2C_{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})\cdot\eta^{2}.\end{array}

(51)

Alternatively, if
\pi^{(t)}
and
\hat{\pi}^{(t)}
are random permutations and generated independently, then, with a similar condition on
\eta
as above, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\right]&\leq&\frac{4}{T\eta}\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}^{0})-\Psi^{\star}_{\gamma}\right]+8L_{\Psi}(2C_{2}\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{n}+\Lambda_{1})\cdot\eta^{2}.\end{array}

(52)

Proof.

From (
47
), and note that
L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}\leq 1
, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{L_{\Psi}\cdot\eta_{t}}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|w_{i-1}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Using (
43
) with the condition
\left(2C_{1}+4C_{2}L_{F}^{2}\right)\eta_{t}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and (
20
) of Assumption
5
, and
w_{0}^{(t)}=\widetilde{w}_{t-1}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|w_{i}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}&\leq&4\eta_{t}^{2}\big[\|\nabla{\Phi_{\gamma}}(w^{(t)}_{0})\|^{2}+2C_{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}\big]\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:grad_mapp_bound}}{\leq}&4\eta_{t}^{2}\left[\Lambda_{0}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+2C_{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1}\right].\end{array}

Combining the two estimates, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{L_{\Psi}\eta_{t}}{2}\cdot 4\eta_{t}^{2}\left[\Lambda_{0}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+2C_{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1}\right]\\
&=&\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\left(1-2L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t}-4L_{\Psi}\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{2}\right)\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+2L_{\Psi}(2C_{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1}\big)\cdot\eta_{t}^{3}\\
&\leq&\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{\eta_{t}}{4}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+2L_{\Psi}(2C_{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1}\big)\cdot\eta_{t}^{3},\end{array}

provided that
4L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t}+8L_{\Psi}\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{2}\leq 1
.
Following the same proof as in
[
33
, Theorem 3]
, we obtain our bound in (
51
).

For the randomized bound, we take expectation and obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\right]&\leq&\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\right]-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\right]+\frac{L_{\Psi}\cdot\eta_{t}}{2n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w_{i-1}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

Using (
44
) with similar argument as the deterministic case, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\|w_{i-1}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}\big]&\leq&4\eta_{t}^{2}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big[\|\nabla{\Phi_{\gamma}}(w^{(t)}_{0})\|^{2}\big]+2C_{2}\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{n}\Big]\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:grad_mapp_bound}}{\leq}&4\eta_{t}^{2}\left[\Lambda_{0}\mathbb{E}\big[\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\big]+2C_{2}\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{n}+\Lambda_{1}\right].\end{array}

Combining the last two estimates, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\right]&\leq&\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\right]-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t})}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\right]\\
&&+{~}\frac{L_{\Psi}\eta_{t}}{2}\cdot 4\eta_{t}^{2}\left[\Lambda_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\right]+2C_{2}\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{n}+\Lambda_{1}\right]\\
&=&\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\right]-\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\left(1-2L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t}-4L_{\Psi}\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{2}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\right]\\
&&+{~}2L_{\Psi}(2C_{2}\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{n}+\Lambda_{1}\big)\cdot\eta_{t}^{3}\\
&\leq&\mathbb{E}\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\right]-\frac{\eta_{t}}{4}\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\right]+2L_{\Psi}(2C_{2}\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{n}+\Lambda_{1}\big)\cdot\eta_{t}^{3},\end{array}

provided that
4L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta_{t}+8L_{\Psi}\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{2}\leq 1
.
Follow the same proof as in
[
33
, Theorem 3]
, we can easily get (
52
).
∎

The following theorem, Theorem
6
is the full version of Theorem
1
in the main text, where the learning rate
\eta
and the number of epochs
T
are given explicitly.

Theorem 6
.

Suppose that Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
, and
5
holds for the setting (NL) of (
1
) and

\begin{array}[]{lcl}Q_{\gamma}:=\frac{M_{F}^{2}\|K\|^{2}}{\mu_{h}+\gamma}+M_{h}L_{F}\|K\|.\end{array}

(53)

Let
\{\widetilde{w}_{t}\}
be generated by Algorithm
1
after
T
epochs using arbitrarily deterministic permutations
\pi^{(t)}
and
\hat{\pi}^{(t)}
and a learning rate
\eta_{t}=\eta>0
such that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\eta:=\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})}}\quad\text{and}\quad T:=\Big\lfloor\frac{16\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})}\cdot[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}+\gamma B_{\phi_{0}}]}{\epsilon^{3}}\Big\rfloor,\end{array}

(54)

for a given sufficiently small tolerance
\epsilon>0
such that
\eta\leq\frac{1}{8Q_{\gamma}}
.
Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}.\end{array}

Alternatively, if
\{\widetilde{w}_{t}\}
is generated by Algorithm
1
after
T
epochs using two random and independent permutations
\pi^{(t)}
and
\hat{\pi}^{(t)}
and a learning rate
\eta_{t}=\eta>0
such that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\eta:=\frac{\sqrt{n}\epsilon}{\sqrt{2Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+n\Lambda_{1})}}\quad\text{and}\quad T:=\Big\lfloor\frac{16\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+n\Lambda_{1})}\cdot[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}+\gamma B_{\phi_{0}}]}{\sqrt{n}\epsilon^{3}}\Big\rfloor,\end{array}

(55)

for a given sufficiently small tolerance
\epsilon>0
such that
\eta\leq\frac{1}{8Q_{\gamma}}
.
Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}]\leq\epsilon^{2}.\end{array}

Proof of Theorem
6
.

Recall that
C_{1}:=\frac{2M_{F}^{4}\|K\|^{4}}{(\mu_{h}+\gamma)^{2}}
and
C_{2}:=2M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}
,
L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}:=M_{h}\|K\|L_{F}+\frac{M_{F}^{2}\|K\|^{2}}{\mu_{h}+\gamma}
, and
L_{\Psi}:=\frac{2M_{F}^{4}\|K\|^{4}}{(\mu_{h}+\gamma)^{2}}+4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}L_{F}^{2}
.
Let us denote by
Q_{\gamma}:=\frac{M_{F}^{2}\|K\|^{2}}{\mu_{h}+\gamma}+M_{h}L_{F}\|K\|
as in Theorem
6
.

In this case, the first conditions
\left(2C_{1}+4C_{2}L_{F}^{2}\right)\eta^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
4L_{\Phi_{\gamma}}\eta+8L_{\Psi}\Lambda_{0}\eta^{2}\leq 1
of Lemma
10
respectively reduce to

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\frac{M_{F}^{4}\|K\|^{4}+2(\mu_{h}+\gamma)^{2}M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}L_{F}^{2}}{(\mu_{h}+\gamma)^{2}}\cdot\eta^{2}\leq\frac{1}{8}\quad\text{and}\\
&\frac{M_{F}^{2}\|K\|^{2}+(\mu_{h}+\gamma)M_{h}\|K\|L_{F}}{\mu_{h}+\gamma}\cdot\eta+\frac{4(M_{F}^{4}\|K\|^{4}+2(\mu_{h}+\gamma)^{2}M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}L_{F}^{2})}{(\mu_{h}+\gamma)^{2}}\cdot\eta^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}.\end{array}

Since

\begin{array}[]{lcl}2\big(M_{F}^{2}\|K\|^{2}+(\mu_{h}+\gamma)M_{h}\|K\|L_{F}\big)^{2}&=&2M_{F}^{4}\|K\|^{4}+2(\mu_{h}+\gamma)^{2}M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}L_{F}^{2}\\
&&+{~}4(\mu_{h}+\gamma)M_{h}\|K\|^{3}L_{F}M_{F}^{2}\\
&\geq&M_{F}^{4}\|K\|^{4}+2(\mu_{h}+\gamma)^{2}M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}L_{F}^{2},\end{array}

the last two conditions hold if
0<\eta\leq\frac{\mu_{h}+\gamma}{8(M_{F}^{2}\|K\|^{2}+(\mu_{h}+\gamma)M_{h}L_{F}\|K\|)}=\frac{1}{8Q_{\gamma}}
.
Moreover, we also have
L_{\Psi}\leq 2Q_{\gamma}
.

Now, from (
51
), to guarantee
\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}
, we impose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{4}{T\eta}\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}^{0})-\Psi^{\star}_{\gamma}\right]+8L_{\Psi}(2C_{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})\cdot\eta^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}.\end{array}

Since
0<\eta\leq\frac{1}{8Q_{\gamma}}
and
L_{\Psi}\leq 2Q_{\gamma}
, we can choose
\eta:=\frac{1}{2}\min\Big\{\frac{1}{4Q_{\gamma}},\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})}}\Big\}
.
Hence, the last inequality holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}T\geq 16\cdot\max\Big\{\frac{\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})}}{\epsilon^{3}},\frac{4Q_{\gamma}}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big\}\cdot\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}^{0})-\Psi^{\star}_{\gamma}\right].\end{array}

By Lemma
3
(c), we can easily show that
\Psi_{\gamma}(w)\leq\Psi_{0}(w)\leq\Psi_{\gamma}(w)+\gamma B_{\phi_{0}}
for any
w
, where
B_{\phi_{0}}:=\sup\{b(u):u\in\mathrm{dom}\left(h\right)\}
.
Hence, we have
\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}^{0})-\Psi^{\star}_{\gamma}\leq\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}+\gamma B_{\phi_{0}}
.
Using this condition, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}T:=\Big\lfloor 16\cdot\max\Big\{\frac{\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})}}{\epsilon^{3}},\frac{4Q_{\gamma}}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big\}\cdot\left[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}+\gamma B_{\phi_{0}}\right]\Big\rfloor.\end{array}

If we choose
\epsilon
sufficiently small such that the
0<\epsilon\leq\frac{\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})}}{4Q_{\gamma}}
, then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\eta:=\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{2Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})}}\quad\text{and}\quad T:=\Big\lfloor\frac{16\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})}\cdot[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}+\gamma B_{\phi_{0}}]}{\epsilon^{3}}\Big\rfloor,\end{array}

as shown in (
54
) of Theorem
6
.

If a random shuffling strategy is used, then to guarantee
\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}]\leq\epsilon^{2}
, from (
52
), we can impose the following condition

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{4}{T\eta}\left[\Psi_{\gamma}(\widetilde{w}^{0})-\Psi^{\star}_{\gamma}\right]+8L_{\Psi}(2C_{2}\frac{\sigma_{J}^{2}}{n}+\Lambda_{1})\cdot\eta^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}.\end{array}

Reasoning the same way as above, we can choose
\eta:=\frac{1}{2}\min\Big\{\frac{1}{4Q_{\gamma}},\frac{\sqrt{n}\epsilon}{\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+n\Lambda_{1})}}\Big\}
.
This leads to the choice of
T
as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}T:=\Big\lfloor 16\cdot\max\Big\{\frac{\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+n\Lambda_{1})}}{\sqrt{n}\epsilon^{3}},\frac{4Q_{\gamma}}{\epsilon^{2}}\Big\}\cdot\left[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}+\gamma B_{\phi_{0}}\right]\Big\rfloor.\end{array}

If we choose
\epsilon
sufficiently small such that the
0<\epsilon\leq\frac{\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+n\Lambda_{1})}}{4Q_{\gamma}\sqrt{n}}
, then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\eta:=\frac{\sqrt{n}\epsilon}{\sqrt{2Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+n\Lambda_{1})}}\quad\text{and}\quad T:=\Big\lfloor\frac{16\sqrt{Q_{\gamma}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+n\Lambda_{1})}\cdot[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}+\gamma B_{\phi_{0}}]}{\sqrt{n}\epsilon^{3}}\Big\rfloor,\end{array}

as shown in (
55
) of Theorem
6
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
1
.

(a) If
h
is
\mu_{h}
-strongly convex with
\mu_{h}>0
, then we can set
\gamma=0
, i.e. without using smoothing technique.
Then, we have
Q_{\gamma}:=\frac{M_{F}^{2}\|K\|^{2}}{\mu_{h}+\gamma}+M_{h}L_{F}\|K\|
reduces to
Q_{0}:=\frac{M_{F}^{2}\|K\|^{2}}{\mu_{h}}+M_{h}L_{F}\|K\|
.

If arbitrary permutations
\pi^{(t)}
and
\hat{\pi}^{(t)}
are used, then
T
from (
54
) reduces to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}T:=\Big\lfloor\frac{16\sqrt{Q_{0}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})}\cdot[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}]}{\epsilon^{3}}\Big\rfloor.\end{array}

Note that, each epoch
t\in[T]
requires either
2n
(for
Option 1
) or
n
(for
Option 2
) evaluations of
F_{i}
and
n
evaluations of
\nabla{F}_{i}
.
Hence, Algorithm
1
requires
\mathcal{O}(n\epsilon^{-3})
evaluations of
F_{i}
and
\mathcal{O}(n\epsilon^{-3})
evaluations of
\nabla{F_{i}}
to achieve an
\epsilon
-stationary point of (
3
).

Alternatively, if
\pi^{(t)}
and
\hat{\pi}^{(t)}
are random and independent permutations, then
T
from (
55
) reduces to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}T:=\Big\lfloor\frac{16\sqrt{Q_{0}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+n\Lambda_{1})}\cdot[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}]}{\sqrt{n}\epsilon^{3}}\Big\rfloor.\end{array}

Clearly, if
\Lambda_{1}=\frac{\Gamma}{n}
for some constant
\Gamma>0
, then plugging this
\Lambda_{1}
into the right-hand side of
T
above, we can conclude that Algorithm
1
requires
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{n}\epsilon^{-3})
evaluations of
F_{i}
and
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{n}\epsilon^{-3})
evaluations of
\nabla{F_{i}}
to achieve an
\epsilon
-stationary point of (
3
).

(b) If
h
is only merely convex, i.e.
\mu_{h}=0
, then we have
Q_{\gamma}=\frac{M_{F}^{2}\|K\|^{2}}{\gamma}+M_{h}L_{F}\|K\|=\mathcal{O}(\gamma^{-1})
.
Moreover, to obtain an
\epsilon
-stationary point of (
3
) from a stationary point of its smoothed problem (
10
), with a similar proof as of Lemma
2
, we need to choose
\gamma:=\epsilon
.
In this case, we get
Q_{\epsilon}=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-1})
.

If arbitrary permutations
\pi^{(t)}
and
\hat{\pi}^{(t)}
are used, then
T
from (
54
) reduces to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}T:=\Big\lfloor\frac{16\sqrt{Q_{\epsilon}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+\Lambda_{1})}\cdot[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}+\epsilon B_{\phi_{0}}]}{\epsilon^{3}}\Big\rfloor=\mathcal{O}\Big(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{7/2}}\Big).\end{array}

Hence, Algorithm
1
requires
\mathcal{O}(n\epsilon^{-7/2})
evaluations of
F_{i}
and
\mathcal{O}(n\epsilon^{-7/2})
evaluations of
\nabla{F_{i}}
to achieve an
\epsilon
-stationary point of (
3
).

Alternatively, if
\pi^{(t)}
and
\hat{\pi}^{(t)}
are random and independent permutations, then
T
from (
55
) reduces to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}T:=\Big\lfloor\frac{16\sqrt{Q_{\epsilon}(4M_{h}^{2}\|K\|^{2}\sigma_{J}^{2}+n\Lambda_{1})}\cdot[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}+\epsilon B_{\phi_{0}}]}{\sqrt{n}\epsilon^{3}}\Big\rfloor.\end{array}

Clearly, if
\Lambda_{1}=\frac{\Gamma}{n}
for some constant
\Gamma>0
, then plugging this
\Lambda_{1}
into the right-hand side of
T
above, we can conclude that Algorithm
1
requires
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{n}\epsilon^{-7/2})
evaluations of
F_{i}
and
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{n}\epsilon^{-7/2})
evaluations of
\nabla{F_{i}}
to achieve an
\epsilon
-stationary point of (
3
).
∎

Remark 1
.

We note that since each epoch
t
of Algorithm
1
requires one evaluation of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{f}f}
, the total number of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
evaluations is
T
.

Appendix C
Convergence Analysis of Algorithm
2
– The NC Setting

In this section, we present the full convergence analysis of Algorithm
2
for both the
semi-shuffling
and the
full-shuffling
variants.

For our notational convenience, we introduce the following function:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\psi(w,u)&:=&-\mathcal{H}(w,u)+h(u).\end{array}

(56)

By Assumption
4
,
\psi(w,\cdot)
is
\mu_{\psi}
-strongly convex with the strong convexity parameter
\mu_{\psi}:=\mu_{h}+\mu_{H}>0
for any
w
such that
(w,u)\in\mathrm{dom}\left(\mathcal{L}\right)
.
Moreover, the Lipschitz constant
\kappa
of
u_{0}^{*}(\cdot)
in Lemma
1
becomes
\kappa:=\frac{L_{u}}{\mu_{h}+\mu_{H}}=\frac{L_{u}}{\mu_{\psi}}>0
.

Furthermore,
\Phi_{0}
and
\Psi_{0}
defined by (
2
) and (
3
), respectively can be expressed as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Phi_{0}(w)&:=&{\displaystyle\max_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{q}}}\big\{\mathcal{H}(w,u)-h(u)\big\}=-{\displaystyle\min_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{q}}}\psi(w,u),\\
\Psi_{0}(w)&:=&f(w)+\Phi_{0}(w)=f(w)+\mathcal{H}(w,u_{0}^{*}(w))-h(u_{0}^{*}(w)),\end{array}

(57)

where
u_{0}^{*}(w):=\mathrm{arg}\!\displaystyle\min_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{q}}\psi(w,u)
is computed by (
2
).

C.1
One-epoch analysis: Key lemmas

We separate the technical lemmas for two variants: the
semi-shuffling variant
using (
26
), and the
full-shuffling variant
using (
27
) into two subsections, respectively.

(a) Key bound for the gradient-ascent scheme (
26
).

If we apply (
26
) to approximate
u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
, then we have the following result.

Lemma 11
.

Suppose that Assumption
4
holds.
Let
\{\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s}\}
be updated by (
26
) such that
0<\hat{\eta}_{t}\leq\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}
.
Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)^{S}\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(58)

Proof.

The proof of Lemma
11
is certainly classical and not new. It can be found in the literature, including
[
31
]
.
However, it may be inconvenient to find a unified proof for the strong convexity of
\mathcal{H}_{i}
and
h
altogether.
Therefore, we present it here for completeness.

For simplicity of our presentation, we denote
\varphi(u):=-\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u)=-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{H}_{i}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u)
and
u^{*}_{t}:=u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
computed by (
2
).

By Assumption
4
,
\varphi
is
\mu_{H}
-strongly convex and
L_{u}
-smooth.
The scheme (
26
) is exactly a proximal gradient method to solve
\min_{u}\{Q(u):=\varphi(u)+h(u)\}
, where
h
is also
\mu_{h}
-strongly convex.
Moreover, by the definition of
\varphi
and of
u^{*}_{t}
, and (
26
), it is obvious to show that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}u^{*}_{t}&=&\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}\big(u^{*}_{t}-\hat{\eta}_{t}\nabla{\varphi}(u^{*}_{t})\big),\\
\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s}&=&\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}\big(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}-\hat{\eta}_{t}\nabla{\varphi}(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1})\big).\end{array}\right.

Hence, by (
32
) from Fact [
F_{1}
], we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}&=&\|\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}\big(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}-\hat{\eta}_{t}\nabla{\varphi}(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1})\big)-\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}\big(u^{*}_{t}-\hat{\eta}_{t}\nabla{\varphi}(u^{*}_{t})\big)\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{1}{1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}-u^{*}_{t}-\hat{\eta}_{t}[\nabla{\varphi}(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1})-\nabla{\varphi}(u^{*}_{t})]\|^{2}.\end{array}

Expanding the right-hand side of the last estimate, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}-u^{*}_{t}-\hat{\eta}_{t}[\nabla{\varphi}(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1})-\nabla{\varphi}(u^{*}_{t})]\|^{2}&=&\|\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}+\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\|\nabla{\varphi}(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1})-\nabla{\varphi}(u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}2\hat{\eta}_{t}\langle\nabla{\varphi}(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1})-\nabla{\varphi}(u^{*}_{t}),\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}-u^{*}_{t}\rangle.\end{array}

Using
[
31
, Theorem 2.1.12]
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle\nabla{\varphi}(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1})-\nabla{\varphi}(u^{*}_{t}),\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}-u^{*}_{t}\rangle&\geq&\frac{L_{u}\mu_{H}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\|\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\|\nabla{\varphi}(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1})-\nabla{\varphi}(u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}.\end{array}

Combining the last three inequalities, we obtain

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{1}{1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}\big(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)\|\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}\big(\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}-\hat{\eta}_{t}\big)\|\nabla{\varphi}(\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1})-\nabla{\varphi}(u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}.\end{array}

Therefore, if
0<\hat{\eta}_{t}\leq\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}
, then the last inequality reduces to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{1}{1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}\big(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)\|\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}.\end{array}

By induction, and noting that
\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{0}:=\widetilde{u}_{t-1}
and
\widetilde{u}_{t}:=\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{S}
, this inequality implies (
58
).
∎

(b) Key bound for the shuffling gradient-ascent scheme (
27
).

Alternatively, if the
full-shuffling variant
(
27
) is used in Algorithm
2
, then we can bound
\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}
for (
27
) as follows.

First, let us define
u_{0}^{s*}:=u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
and for all
i\in[n]
:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}u_{i}^{s*}&:=&u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}_{\pi^{(s,t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})).\end{array}

(59)

Here,
\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
is the partial derivative (or the gradient) of
\mathcal{H}_{i}
w.r.t.
u
.

Next, we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 12
.

Suppose that Assumption
4
holds, and
u_{i}^{s*}
is defined by (
59
) for all
i=0,\cdots,n
.
Then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|u^{s*}_{i}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{2\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\cdot i}{n}\cdot\big(\Theta_{u}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big)+\frac{2\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\cdot i^{2}}{n^{2}}\cdot\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&\leq&2\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\big[(\Theta_{u}+1)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big].\end{array}

(60)

Proof.

For simplicity, we denote
u^{*}_{t}:=u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
.
For
i=0
, we obviously have
\|u_{0}^{s*}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}=0
, showing that (
60
) trivially holds.

Next, for
i\in[n]
, using
u_{i}^{s*}
from (
59
) and Young’s inequality twice in
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
and
{\tiny\textcircled{2}}
, we can derive that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|u_{i}^{s*}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}&=&\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\sum_{j=1}^{i}\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}_{\pi^{(s)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&\overset{{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\leq}&\frac{2\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\cdot i^{2}\cdot\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big[\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}_{\pi^{(s)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})-\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})\big]\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{2\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\cdot i^{2}\|\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&\overset{{\tiny\textcircled{2}}}{\leq}&\frac{2i\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big\|\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}_{\pi^{(s)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})-\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}+\frac{2i^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:exact_grad_phi}}{\leq}&\frac{2i\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\big\|\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}_{\pi^{(s)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})-\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}+\frac{2i^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

By (
13
) from Assumption
4
and (
5
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\big\|\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}_{\pi^{(s)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})-\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:bounded_variance2}}{\leq}&\Theta_{u}\|\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:exact_grad_phi}}{=}&\Theta_{u}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}.\end{array}

Combining the last two inequalities, and noting that
0\leq i\leq n
, we obtain (
60
).
∎

Finally, we can prove the necessary bound for
\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}
.
For simplicity of our proof, let us denote
g^{s,t}_{i-1}(\cdot):=-\mathcal{H}_{\pi^{(s)}(i)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\cdot)
and again
u^{*}_{t}:=u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
.
By Assumption
4
(a) and (b), it is clear that
g^{s,t}_{i-1}(\cdot)
is
\mu_{H}
-strongly convex and
L_{u}
-smooth.
Let us consider the following the Bregman distance constructed from
g_{i-1}^{s,t}
:

D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u,\hat{u})=g^{s,t}_{i-1}(u)-g^{s,t}_{i-1}(\hat{u})-\langle\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(\hat{u}),u-\hat{u}\rangle.

(61)

The following lemma is adapted from Theorems 2 and 3 in
[
30
]
with some modification.

Lemma 13
.

Suppose that Assumption
4
holds.
Let
u_{i}^{s*}
be defined by (
59
),
\{u_{i}^{(s,t)}\}
be updated by (
27
) at the
s
-th epoch for all
i\in[n]
, and
D^{s,t}_{i-1}
be defined by (
61
).
Then, it holds that

\hskip 0.0pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|u_{i}^{(s,t)}-u_{i}^{s*}\|^{2}&\leq&\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)\|u^{(s,t)}_{i-1}-u_{i-1}^{s*}\|^{2}+\frac{2L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}}{n}\big[(\Theta_{u}+1)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big]\\
&&-{~}\frac{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big(1-\frac{L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})).\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(62)

Consequently, at each epoch
s
, the following bound holds:

\hskip-4.30554pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widehat{u}_{s}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{1}{1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{n}\|\widehat{u}_{s-1}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{2L_{u}\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}}{n(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\big[\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{j}\big]\big[(\Theta_{u}+1)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big].\end{array}\hskip-21.52771pt

(63)

If we update (
27
) by
S
epochs starting from
\widehat{u}_{0}^{(t)}:=\widetilde{u}_{t-1}
and output
\widetilde{u}_{t}:=\widehat{u}_{S}^{(t)}
, then

\hskip 0.0pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{nS}\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{2L_{u}}{n}C_{S}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}\cdot\big[(\Theta_{u}+1)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big],\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

(64)

where
C_{S}:=\big[\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{j}\big]\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{s}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{ns}
.

Proof.

By (
27
), using the definition of
g_{i-1}^{s,t}(\cdot)
above, and
u_{i}^{s*}
defined by (
59
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}u_{i}^{(s,t)}&=&u_{0}^{(s,t)}+\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}_{\pi^{(s)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{j-1}^{(s,t)})=u_{0}^{(s,t)}-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{j-1}}(u_{j-1}^{(s,t)})\\
&=&u_{i-1}^{(s,t)}-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)})\\
u_{i}^{s*}&=&u^{*}_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{j-1}}(u_{t}^{*})=u_{i-1}^{s*}-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(u_{t}^{*}).\end{array}

Using these expressions, for any
i\in[n]
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|u_{i}^{(s,t)}-u_{i}^{s*}\|^{2}&=&\|u^{(s,t)}_{i-1}-u_{i-1}^{s*}\|^{2}-\frac{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\langle\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)})-\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(u^{*}_{t}),u_{i-1}^{(s,t)}-u_{i-1}^{s*}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)})-\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(65)

By the
L
-smoothness condition (
12
) of
\mathcal{H}_{i}
from Assumption
4
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)})-\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(u^{*}_{t})\|^{2}&\leq&2L_{u}D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)},u^{*}_{t}).\end{array}

(66)

By the well-known three-point identity, see, e.g.,
[
8
]
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\langle\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)})-\nabla_{u}{g^{s,t}_{i-1}}(u^{*}_{t}),u_{i-1}^{(s,t)}-u_{i-1}^{s*}\rangle&=&D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)},u^{s*}_{i-1})+D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)},u^{*}_{t})\\
&&-{~}D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{s*},u^{*}_{t}).\end{array}

Substituting this inequality and (
66
) into (
65
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|u_{i}^{(s.t)}-u_{i}^{s*}\|^{2}&\leq&\|u^{(s,t)}_{i-1}-u_{i-1}^{s*}\|^{2}-\frac{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)},u^{s*}_{i-1})+\frac{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{s*},u^{*}_{t})\\
&&-{~}\frac{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big(1-\frac{L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)},u^{*}_{t}).\end{array}

On the one hand, by the
\mu_{H}
-strong-convexity of
g_{i-1}^{s,t}
, we have
D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)},u^{s*}_{i-1})\geq\frac{\mu_{H}}{2}\|u^{(s,t)}_{i-1}-u_{i-1}^{s*}\|^{2}
.
On the other hand, by the
L_{u}
-smoothness of
g_{i-1}^{s,t}
, we also have
D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{s*},u^{*}_{t})\leq\frac{L_{u}}{2}\|u_{i-1}^{s*}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}
.
Using these bounds into the last inequality, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|u_{i}^{(s,t)}-u_{i}^{s*}\|^{2}&\leq&\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)\|u^{(s,t)}_{i-1}-u_{i-1}^{s*}\|^{2}+\frac{L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\|u_{i-1}^{s*}-u^{*}_{t}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big(1-\frac{L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)},u^{*}_{t}).\end{array}

Combining this inequality and (
60
), we obtain (
62
).

Next, since
1-\frac{L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\geq 0
and
D^{s,t}_{i-1}(u_{i-1}^{(s,t)},u^{*}_{t})\geq 0
, we obtain from (
62
) that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|u_{i}^{(s,t)}-u_{i}^{s*}\|^{2}&\leq&\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)\|u^{(s,t)}_{i-1}-u_{i-1}^{s*}\|^{2}+2n^{2}L_{u}\big[(\Theta_{u}+1)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big]\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}.\end{array}

By induction, rolling this inequality from
i=1
to
n
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|u_{n}^{(s,t)}-u_{n}^{s*}\|^{2}&\leq&\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{n}\|u^{(s,t)}_{0}-u_{0}^{s*}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{2L_{u}}{n}[(\Theta_{u}+1)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}]\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{j}.\end{array}

(67)

Next, from (
59
) and (
33
), it is not hard to show that
u^{*}_{t}=u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})=\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}(u_{n}^{s*})
.
Furthermore, by the second line of (
27
), we also have
\widehat{u}_{s}^{(t)}=\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}(u_{n}^{(s,t)})
.
Since
h
is
\mu_{h}
-strongly convex, by (
32
) from Fact [
F_{1}
], we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widehat{u}_{s}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}=\|\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}(u_{n}^{(s,t)})-\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}(u_{n}^{s*})\|^{2}\leq\frac{1}{1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|u_{n}^{(s,t)}-u_{n}^{s*}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Using this inequality,
u_{0}^{(s,t)}=\widehat{u}^{(t)}_{s-1}
, and
u_{0}^{s*}=u_{t}^{*}=u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
, it follows from (
67
) that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widehat{u}_{s}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{1}{1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{n}\|\widehat{u}_{s-1}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{2L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}}{n(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\cdot\big[\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{j}\big]\cdot\big[(\Theta_{u}+1)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big],\end{array}

which proves (
63
).

Next, rolling (
63
) from
s=1
to
S
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widehat{u}_{s}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{nS}\|\widehat{u}_{0}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{2L_{u}}{n}C_{S}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}\cdot\big[(\Theta_{u}+1)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big],\end{array}

where
C_{S}:=\big[\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{j}\big]\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{s}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{ns}
.
Substituting
\widehat{u}_{0}^{(t)}:=\widetilde{u}_{t-1}
and
\widetilde{u}_{t}:=\widehat{u}_{S}^{(t)}
into the last inequality, it proves (
64
).
∎

(c) Key bounds for the shuffling gradient descent scheme (
28
).

We define the following quantity:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}g_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w^{(t)}_{j-1},\widetilde{u}_{t}).\end{array}

(68)

From the update of
w_{i}^{(t)}
in (
28
), for any
i\in[n]
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}w_{i}^{(t)}&=&w_{0}^{(t)}-\frac{\eta_{t}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t}).\end{array}

(69)

Then, it is obvious that
w_{n}^{(t)}=w_{0}^{(t)}-\eta_{t}g_{t}
.

First, we bound
\Delta_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\|w_{i}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}
for (
28
) to handle the upper-level problem (
3
).

Lemma 14
.

Suppose that Assumption
4
holds.
Let
\{w_{i}^{(t)}\}
be generated by (
28
) such that
w_{0}^{(t)}:=\widetilde{w}_{t-1}
.
Then, if we choose
\eta_{t}>0
such that
1-3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\geq 0
, then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Delta_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\|w_{i}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}&\leq&2(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{2}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+6\eta_{t}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}\\
&&+{~}4L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(70)

Let
g_{t}
be defined by (
68
) and
\Phi_{0}
be defined by (
2
).
Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g_{t}-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{L_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\|w_{i}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}\equiv L_{w}^{2}\Delta_{t},\\
\|\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla{\Phi}_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&L_{u}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2},\\
\|g_{t}-\nabla{\Phi}_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&L_{w}^{2}\Delta_{t}+L_{u}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(71)

Proof.

Utilizing (
69
) and Young’s inequality in
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
and
{\tiny\textcircled{2}}
below, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|w_{i}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM2_w_update_upto_i}}{=}&\frac{i^{2}\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&\overset{{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\leq}&\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\big\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big[\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{0}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\big]\big\|^{2}+\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\big\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big[\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{0}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})\big]\big\|^{2}\\
&\overset{{\tiny\textcircled{2}}}{\leq}&\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\big\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big[\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{0}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\big]\big\|^{2}+\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{3i\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big\|\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{0}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})\big\|^{2}.\end{array}

Let us denote
\Delta_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\|w_{j}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\|w_{j}^{(t)}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}
.
Then, by (
12
) of Assumption
4
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big\|\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{0}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})\big\|^{2}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:Lsmooth2}}{\leq}&\frac{L_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\|w^{(t)}_{j-1}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}\leq L_{w}^{2}\Delta_{t}.\end{array}

Next, by Young’s inequality again in
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
,
w_{0}^{(t)}=\widetilde{w}_{t-1}
, and (
12
) and (
13
) from Assumption
4
, and the fact that
\nabla_{w}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))=\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
from (
5
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[2]}&:=&\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big[\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{0}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\big]\big\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\textcircled{1}}{\leq}&\frac{2}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big\|\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{0}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla_{w}\mathcal{H}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w^{(t)}_{0},u_{0}^{*}(w^{(t)}_{0}))\big\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{2}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big\|\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{0}^{(t)},u_{0}^{*}(w^{(t)}_{0}))-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(w_{0}^{(t)},u_{0}^{*}(w^{(t)}_{0}))\big\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:Lsmooth2}}{\leq}&\frac{2}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{2L_{u}^{2}}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:bounded_variance2},\eqref{eq:exact_grad_phi}}{\leq}&2L_{u}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{2n}{i}\big[\Theta_{w}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{w}^{2}\big].\end{array}

Combining three inequalities above, we arrive at

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|w_{i}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{6i^{2}\cdot L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{6i\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n}\big[\Theta_{w}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{w}^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{3i\cdot L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{n}\Delta_{t}\\
&=&\frac{6i^{2}\cdot L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{3i\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}(2n\Theta_{w}+i)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{6i\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n}\sigma_{w}^{2}+\frac{3i\cdot L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{n}\Delta_{t}.\end{array}

Averaging this inequality from
i=0
to
n-1
, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Delta_{t}&:=&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\|w_{i}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\big[\frac{6i^{2}\cdot L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{3i\eta_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}(2n\Theta_{w}+i)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\big[\frac{6i\cdot\eta_{t}^{2}}{n}\sigma_{w}^{2}+\frac{3i\cdot L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{n}\Delta_{t}\big]\\
&\leq&2L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{2}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+3\eta_{t}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}+\frac{3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{2}\Delta_{t}.\end{array}

Here, we have used the facts that
\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}i=\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\leq\frac{n^{2}}{2}
and
\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}i^{2}=\frac{n(n-1)(2n-1)}{6}\leq\frac{n^{3}}{3}
.
Rearranging the last inequality, we obtain (
70
).

Finally, to prove (
71
), we proceed as follows.
Using (
68
) and (
12
) from Assumption
4
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g_{t}-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})\|^{2}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM2_gt_quantity}}{=}&\big\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\big[\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})\big]\big\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\big\|\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{0}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})\big\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:Lsmooth2}}{\leq}&\frac{L_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|w_{j-1}^{(t)}-w^{(t)}_{0}\|^{2},\end{array}

which proves the first line of (
71
).

We also note that
\nabla{\Phi}_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))
due to (
5
).
Using this expression, and (
12
) from Assumption
4
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla{\Phi}_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&=&\big\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))\big]\big\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\|\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))\big\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:Lsmooth2}}{\leq}&\frac{L_{u}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}=L_{u}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2},\end{array}

which proves the second line of (
71
).

Similarly, combining (
68
), (
5
), and (
12
) from Assumption
4
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g_{t}-\nabla{\Phi}_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM2_gt_quantity},\eqref{eq:exact_grad_phi}}{=}&\big\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\big[\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))\big]\big\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\big\|\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))\big\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:Lsmooth2}}{\leq}&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\big[L_{w}^{2}\|w_{j-1}^{(t)}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}+L_{u}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\big],\end{array}

which proves the third line of (
71
).
∎

Lemma 15
.

Suppose that Assumption
4
holds.
Let
\{w_{i}^{(t)}\}
be generated by (
28
),
g_{t}
be defined by (
68
),
\Psi
be defined by (
3
), and
{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}
be defined by (
18
).
Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(72)

Proof.

Let us denote
\widehat{w}_{t}:=\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}\big(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-\eta_{t}\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\big)
.
Then, from (
18
), we can easily show that
\mathcal{G}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})=\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-\widehat{w}_{t})
.
Therefore, we have
\nabla{f}(\widehat{w}_{t}):=\eta_{t}^{-1}\big(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-\widehat{w}_{t}\big)-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\in\partial{f}(\widehat{w}_{t})
.
By the convexity of
f
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}f(\widehat{w}_{t})&\leq&f(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\langle\nabla{f}(\widehat{w}_{t}),\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\rangle\\
&=&f(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\langle\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}),\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\rangle-\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}\|\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Next, by the
L_{\Phi_{0}}
-smoothness of
\Phi
from (
36
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Phi_{0}(\widehat{w}_{t})&\leq&\Phi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\langle\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}),\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\rangle+\frac{L_{\Phi_{0}}}{2}\|\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Adding the last two inequalities together and using
\Psi_{0}(w)=f(w)+\Phi_{0}(w)
from (
3
) and
\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}=-\eta_{t}{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
, we can derive

\hskip 0.0pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widehat{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{(2-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}=\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{\eta_{t}(2-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(73)

Now, from (
69
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}g_{t}:=\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-w_{n}^{(t)})=\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(w_{0}^{(t)}-w_{n}^{(t)})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t}).\end{array}

(74)

Since
\widetilde{w}_{t}=\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}(w_{n}^{(t)})
from the second line of (
28
), we get
\nabla{f}(\widetilde{w}_{t}):=\eta_{t}^{-1}\big(w_{n}^{(t)}-\widetilde{w}_{t}\big)=-g_{t}-\eta_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\in\partial{f}(\widetilde{w}_{t})
.
Hence, again by the convexity of
f
, we can deduce that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}f(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&f(\widehat{w}^{t})+\langle\nabla{f}(\widetilde{w}_{t}),\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle=f(\widehat{w}^{t})-\langle g_{t},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle-\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}\langle\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle\\
&=&f(\widehat{w}^{t})-\langle g_{t},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle-\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\big[\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}+\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\|^{2}-\|\widehat{w}^{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\big].\end{array}

Again, by the
L_{\Phi_{0}}
-smoothness of
\Phi
from (
36
), we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Phi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Phi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\langle\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}),\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\rangle+\frac{L_{\Phi_{0}}}{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2},\\
\Phi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})&\leq&\Phi_{0}(\widehat{w}^{t})+\langle\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}),\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle+\frac{L_{\Phi_{0}}}{2}\|\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Adding the last three inequalities together, and using
\Psi_{0}(w)=f(w)+\Phi_{0}(w)
from (
3
) and
\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}=-\eta_{t}{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
, we can prove that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widehat{w}^{t})+\langle\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-g_{t},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{(1+L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widehat{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\leq}&\Psi_{0}(\widehat{w}^{t})+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-g_{t}\|^{2}-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta_{t}(1+L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2},\end{array}

(75)

where we have used Young’s inequality in the last line
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
as
\langle\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-g_{t},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\rangle\leq\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widehat{w}^{t}\|^{2}
.

Finally, summing up (
73
) and (
75
) we arrive at

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2},\end{array}

which proves (
72
).
∎

C.2
Convergence of the semi-shuffling variant of Algorithm
2

We now prove the convergence of the semi-shuffling variant of Algorithm
2
using (
26
).

Lemma 16
.

Suppose that Assumptions
4
and
5
hold for (
1
).
Let
\Psi
be defined by (
3
) and
{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}
be defined by (
18
).
Let
\{(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})\}
be generated by the
semi-shuffling variant
of Algorithm
2
using (
26
).
For a fixed
\omega>0
, suppose that we choose
\eta_{t}
and
\hat{\eta}_{t}
such that
1-3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\geq 0
and
0<\hat{\eta}_{t}\leq\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}
, and the following conditions hold:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}&2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}+2\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\leq 1,\\
&\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)^{S}\big(1+\omega+\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+2L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big)\leq\omega.\end{array}\right.

(76)

Then, the following bound holds:

\hskip 0.0pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&+&\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\leq\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta_{t}B_{t}}{2}\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\big[3L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}+L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{1}\big]\cdot\eta_{t}^{3},\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

(77)

where
B_{t}:=1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}-2L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{2}
.

Proof.

First, combining (
72
) and the last line of (
71
), we can derive

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|w_{j-1}^{(t)}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}+\frac{L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(78)

Next, substituting (
70
) into (
78
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}+3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{3}\sigma_{w}^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\big(1+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big)\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{3}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

By (
35
) and Young’s inequality in
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
, for any
s_{t}>0
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}&\overset{{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\leq}&(1+s_{t})\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{(1+s_{t})}{s_{t}}\|u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:u_star_smoothness}}{\leq}&(1+s_{t})\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{(1+s_{t})\kappa^{2}}{s_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Multiplying this inequality by
\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}
for some
\omega>0
and adding the result to the last estimate yields

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[1]}&:=&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})+\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\big[1+\omega(1+s_{t})+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big]\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\big[\frac{1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}-\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}(1+s_{t})}{2s_{t}}\big]\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{3}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{3}\sigma_{w}^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{SGM2_bound_u1_dist1}}{\leq}&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)^{S}\big[1+\omega(1+s_{t})+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big]\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\big[\frac{1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}-\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}(1+s_{t})}{2s_{t}}\big]\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{3}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{3}\sigma_{w}^{2}.\end{array}

We need to choose the parameters
\eta_{t}
,
\hat{\eta}_{t}
, and
s_{t}
such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)^{S}\big[1+\omega(1+s_{t})+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big]\leq\omega,\\
&\frac{1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}}{\eta_{t}}-\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}(1+s_{t})}{s_{t}}\geq 0.\end{array}\right.

The second condition leads to
\frac{1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}-\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{s_{t}}
, or equivalently
0<s_{t}\leq\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}-\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}
.
If
2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}+2\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\leq 1
as stated in the first line of (
76
), then we can choose
s_{t}:=2\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}
.
In this case, the second condition is satisfied, while the first condition becomes

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)^{S}\big(1+\omega+2\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big)\leq\omega,\end{array}

which is exactly the second condition of (
76
).

By (
20
) from Assumption
5
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[1]}&:=&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})+\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{3}\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\big[3L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}+L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{1}\big]\eta_{t}^{3}.\end{array}

Rearranging this inequality, we prove (
77
).
∎

The following theorem, Theorem
7
, is the full version of Theorem
2
in the main text, where the learning rates
\eta_{t}
and
\hat{\eta}_{t}
, and the numbers of epochs
S
and
T
are given explicitly.

Theorem 7
.

Suppose that Assumptions
1
,
2
,
4
, and
5
hold for (
1
).
Let
\Psi_{0}
be defined by (
3
), and
{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}
be defined by (
18
).
Let
C_{0}
and
C_{w}
be two constants given as follows:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}C_{0}&:=&2\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\quad\text{and}\quad C_{w}:=L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{1}+3L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}.\end{array}

(79)

Let
\{(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})\}
be generated by Algorithm
2
using the
gradient ascent scheme (
26
)
, and fixed learning rates
\eta_{t}:=\eta>0
and
\hat{\eta}_{t}:=\hat{\eta}\in\big(0,\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big]
such that for a fixed
\omega>0
:

\begin{array}[]{lll}S:=\big\lfloor\frac{M_{\omega}(\eta)}{2\hat{\eta}}\big(\mu_{h}+\frac{4\mu_{H}L_{u}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)^{-1}\big\rfloor\quad\text{and}\quad 0<\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{2\sqrt{C_{0}}},\frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}},\frac{1}{2\omega L_{u}\kappa}\right\},\end{array}

(80)

where
M_{\omega}(\eta):=\frac{1}{\omega}+\big(\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+\frac{2L_{w}^{2}}{\omega}\big)\eta^{2}
.
Then, the following estimate holds:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{4\big[2(\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star})+\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta\|\widetilde{u}^{0}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{0})\|^{2}\big]}{\eta(T+1)}+8C_{w}\eta^{2}.\end{array}

(81)

For a given
\epsilon>0
, if we choose
\eta:=\frac{s\epsilon}{4\sqrt{C_{w}}}
for a fixed
s\in(0,1)
satisfying (
76
),
\hat{\eta}\in\big(0,\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{h}}\big]
, and
T:=\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\big)
, then
\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}
.

For a given
\hat{\eta}\in\big(0,\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{h}}\big]
, we denote
B_{0}:=\hat{\eta}\big(\mu_{h}+\frac{4\mu_{H}L_{u}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)
.
If we choose
\omega:=\frac{1}{B_{0}}
and

\begin{array}[]{lll}0<\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{2\sqrt{C_{0}}},\frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}},\frac{B_{0}}{\sqrt{L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+2B_{0}^{2}L_{w}^{2}}}\right\},\end{array}

(82)

then we have
S=1
, i.e. we only need to perform one iteration of the
gradient ascent scheme (
26
)
.

Consequently, Algorithm
2
requires
\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{n}{\epsilon^{3}}\big)
evaluations of
\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
and of
\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
, and
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
evaluations of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
and of
\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}
to achieve an
\epsilon
-stationary point
\widehat{w}_{T}
of (
1
) computed by (
19
).

Proof of Theorem
7
.

Let us choose
\eta_{t}:=\eta
such that
\eta
satisfies (
80
).
Then, it is obvious to verify that
1-3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\geq 0
and
2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}+2\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\leq 1
.
Moreover, we have
\eta_{t}=\eta\leq\frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}}
,
\eta_{t}=\eta\leq\frac{1}{2L_{w}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}L_{w}}
, and
\eta_{t}=\eta\leq\frac{1}{2\omega L_{u}\kappa}
.
Using these bounds, we can further lower bound
B_{t}:=1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}-2L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{2}
from Lemma
16
as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}B_{t}&\geq&\frac{1}{2}-2\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta^{2}.\end{array}

Now, we need to choose
0<\eta\leq\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)}}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{C_{0}}}
so that
B_{t}\geq\frac{1}{4}
, where
C_{0}
is given in (
79
).
Moreover, the second condition of (
76
) holds if

\begin{array}[]{lclcl}&&\frac{1}{(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)^{S}&\leq&\frac{\omega}{1+\omega+(\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+2L_{w}^{2})\eta^{2}},\\
&\Leftrightarrow&S\ln(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta})-S\ln\big(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)&\geq&\ln\big(1+\frac{1}{\omega}+(\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+\frac{2L_{w}^{2}}{\omega})\eta^{2}\big).\end{array}

Using the elementary facts
-\ln(1-\tau)\geq\tau
and
\tau\geq\ln(1+\tau)\geq\frac{e}{2}
for all
\tau\in(0,1/2]
, we can show that the last inequality holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}S\hat{\eta}\big(\frac{\mu_{h}}{2}+\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)\geq\frac{1}{\omega}+(\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+\frac{2L_{w}^{2}}{\omega})\eta^{2}.\end{array}

Simplifying this condition, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}S\geq\frac{M_{\omega}(\eta)}{2\hat{\eta}}\big(\mu_{h}+\frac{4\mu_{H}L_{u}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)^{-1},\quad\text{where}\quad M_{\omega}(\eta):=\frac{1}{\omega}+\big(\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+\frac{2L_{w}^{2}}{\omega}\big)\eta^{2}.\end{array}

Clearly, this leads to the choice of
S
as in (
80
).

Let us define
C_{w}:=3L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}+L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{1}
as in (
79
).
Then, (
77
) reduces to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})+\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta}{8}\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+C_{w}\cdot\eta^{3}.\end{array}

Subtracting
\Psi_{0}^{\star}
from both sides of this inequality, and averaging the result from
t=0
to
t=T
, and noting that
\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{T})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}\geq 0
, we obtain (
81
).

Without loss of generality, let us choose
\omega:=1
.
We also choose
\hat{\eta}\in\big(0,\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{h}}\big]
.
Then, we have
M_{\omega}=1+(L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+2L_{w}^{2})\eta^{2}\leq 2
.
Moreover, from (
80
), we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}S=\Big\lfloor\frac{M_{\omega}(\eta)}{2\hat{\eta}}\big[\mu_{h}+\frac{4\mu_{H}L_{u}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big]^{-1}\Big\rfloor\leq\bar{S}:=\Big\lfloor\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}}\big[\mu_{h}+\frac{4\mu_{H}L_{u}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big]^{-1}\Big\rfloor=\mathcal{O}(1).\end{array}

To achieve an
\epsilon
-stationary point of (
3
), from (
81
) we need to impose the following condition:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{2[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}]}{\eta(T+1)}+\frac{L_{u}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}^{0}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{0})\|^{2}}{T+1}+4C_{w}\eta^{2}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4}.\end{array}

If we choose
\eta:=\frac{s\epsilon}{4\sqrt{C_{w}}}
for some
s\in(0,1)
satisfying (
80
), then the last inequality leads to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}T\geq\bar{T}:=\Big\lfloor\frac{32\sqrt{C_{w}}[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}]}{s(1-s^{2})\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{4L_{u}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}^{0}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{0})\|^{2}}{(1-s^{2})\epsilon^{2}}\Big\rfloor=\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\big).\end{array}

Therefore, we can choose
T:=\bar{T}=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
.
Since each iteration
t
, we run
S
epochs of the shuffling scheme (
26
), the total number of evaluations of
\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
is
\mathcal{T}_{u}:=T\times S\times n
.
However, since
1\leq S\leq\bar{S}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we get
\mathcal{T}_{u}:=\mathcal{O}(n\epsilon^{-3})
.
The total number of evaluations of
\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
is
\mathcal{T}_{w}:=Tn=\mathcal{O}(n\epsilon^{-3})
as stated.

Since each epoch
t
, Algorithm
2
requires one evaluation of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
, and
S
evaluations of
\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}
, but since
S=\mathcal{O}(1)
, the total number of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
evaluations is
T=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
, while the total number of
\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}
evaluations is
TS=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
.
Overall, Algorithm
2
needs
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
evaluations of both
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
and
\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}
.

Finally, to perform only one iteration the
gradient ascent scheme (
26
)
at each epoch
t
, we need to choose
\omega
such that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{M_{\omega}(\eta)}{2\hat{\eta}}\big(\mu_{h}+\frac{4\mu_{H}L_{u}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big)^{-1}\leq S=1.\end{array}

This condition leads to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}M_{\omega}(\eta)=\frac{1}{\omega}+\big(\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+\frac{2L_{w}^{2}}{\omega}\big)\eta^{2}\leq 2\hat{\eta}\big(\mu_{h}+\frac{4\mu_{H}L_{u}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big).\end{array}

For a given
\hat{\eta}\in\big(0,\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{h}}\big]
, let us choose
\frac{1}{\omega}:=\hat{\eta}\big(\mu_{h}+\frac{4\mu_{H}L_{u}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big):=B_{0}
.
Then, the last condition becomes
(L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+2B_{0}^{2}L_{w}^{2})\eta^{2}\leq B_{0}^{2}
, or equivalently
\eta\leq\frac{B_{0}}{\sqrt{L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+2B_{0}^{2}L_{w}^{2}}}
.
Combining this condition and (
80
), we get (
82
), i.e.:

\begin{array}[]{lll}0<\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{2\sqrt{C_{0}}},\frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}},\frac{B_{0}}{\sqrt{L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+2B_{0}^{2}L_{w}^{2}}}\right\},\quad\text{where}\quad B_{0}:=\hat{\eta}\big(\mu_{h}+\frac{4\mu_{H}L_{u}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\big).\end{array}

Thus we have
S=1
, i.e. we need to perform only one iteration of (
26
) per epoch
t
.
∎

C.3
Convergence of the full-shuffling variant of Algorithm
2
– The case
S>1

We can combine the results above to obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 17
.

Suppose that Assumptions
4
and
5
hold for (
1
),
\Psi
be defined by (
3
), and
{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}
be defined by (
18
).
Let
\{(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})\}
be generated by the full-shuffling variant of Algorithm
2
using (
27
).
For a fixed
\omega>0
, assume that
\eta_{t}
and
\hat{\eta}_{t}
are chosen such that
1-3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\geq 0
and

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}&2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}+2\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\leq 1,\\
&\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{nS}\big(1+\omega+2\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big)\leq\omega.\end{array}\right.

(83)

Then, the following bound holds:

\hskip 0.0pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&+&\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\leq\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta_{t}B_{t}}{2}\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\big[3L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}+L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{1}\big]\cdot\eta_{t}^{3}\\
&&+{~}\frac{L_{u}^{3}}{n}(1+\omega+2\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2})\big[\Lambda_{1}(\Theta_{u}+1)+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big]\cdot C_{S}\eta_{t}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3},\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

(84)

where
B_{t}:=1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}-2L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{2}-\frac{2L_{u}^{3}}{n}(1+\omega+2\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2})\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}\cdot C_{S}\cdot\Lambda_{0}(\Theta_{u}+1)
for
C_{S}
given in Lemma
13
.

Proof.

First, combining (
78
) and (
70
), we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}+3L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{3}\\
&&+{~}\frac{L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\big(1+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big)\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{3}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

By (
35
) and Young’s inequality in
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
, for any
s_{t}>0
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}&\overset{{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\leq}&(1+s_{t})\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{(1+s_{t})}{s_{t}}\|u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:u_star_smoothness}}{\leq}&(1+s_{t})\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{(1+s_{t})\kappa^{2}}{s_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Multiplying this inequality by
\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}
for some
\omega>0
and adding the result to the last estimate, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[1]}&:=&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})+\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\big[1+\omega(1+s_{t})+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big)\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\big[\frac{1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}-\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}(1+s_{t})}{2s_{t}}\big]\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{3}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{3}\sigma_{w}^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM2_key_est_for_ut2c}}{\leq}&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{nS}\big[1+\omega(1+s_{t})+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big]\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\big[\frac{1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}-\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}(1+s_{t})}{2s_{t}}\big]\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{3}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{3}\sigma_{w}^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{L_{u}^{3}}{n}\big[1+\omega(1+s_{t})+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big]\eta_{t}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}\cdot C_{S}\cdot\big[(\Theta_{u}+1)\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big].\end{array}

We need to choose the parameters
\eta_{t}
,
\hat{\eta}_{t}
, and
s_{t}
such that

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{Sn}\big[1+\omega(1+s_{t})+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big]\leq\omega\\
&\frac{1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}-\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}(1+s_{t})}{2s_{t}}\geq 0.\end{array}

The second one leads to
\frac{1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}-\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{s_{t}}
, or equivalently
0<s_{t}\leq\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}{1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}-\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}
.
If
2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}+2\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\leq 1
as stated in the first line of (
83
), then we can choose
s_{t}:=2\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}
.
In this case, the second condition above holds, and the first condition becomes

\begin{array}[]{ll}&\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{S}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{nS}\big(1+\omega+2\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\big)\leq\omega,\end{array}

which is exactly the second line of (
83
).

By (
20
) from Assumption
5
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[1]}&:=&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})+\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{\omega L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-u^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{3}\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\big[3L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}+L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{1}\big]\eta_{t}^{3}\\
&&+{~}\frac{L_{u}^{3}}{n}(1+\omega+2\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2})\eta_{t}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}\cdot C_{S}\cdot\Lambda_{0}(\Theta_{u}+1)\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{L_{u}^{3}}{n}(1+\omega+2\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2})\eta_{t}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}\cdot C_{S}\big[\Lambda_{1}(\Theta_{u}+1)+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big].\end{array}

Rearranging this inequality, we prove (
84
).
∎

The following theorem, Theorem
8
, is the full version of Theorem
3
in the main text, where the learning rates
\eta_{t}
and
\hat{\eta}_{t}
, and the numbers of epochs
S
and
T
are given explicitly.

Theorem 8

(Strong convexity of
\mathcal{H}_{i}
)
.

Suppose that Assumptions
1
,
2
,
4
, and
5
hold for (
1
), and
\mathcal{H}_{i}
is
\mu_{H}
-strongly concave with
\mu_{H}>0
for all
i\in[n]
, but
h
is only merely convex.
Let
\Psi_{0}
be defined by (
3
), and
{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}
be defined by (
18
).
We define
C_{w}
and
C_{u}
respectively as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}C_{w}&:=&L_{w}^{2}\big[(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{1}+3\sigma_{w}^{2}\big]\quad\text{and}\quad C_{u}:=\frac{7L_{u}^{3}}{2\mu_{H}}\big[\Lambda_{1}(\Theta_{u}+1)+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big].\end{array}

(85)

Let
\{(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})\}
be generated by Algorithm
2
using
S
epochs of
shuffling routine (
27
)
, and fixed learning rates
\eta_{t}:=\eta>0
and
\hat{\eta}_{t}:=\hat{\eta}
such that

\begin{array}[]{lll}S:=\big\lfloor\frac{\ln(7/2)}{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}}\big\rfloor,\qquad 0<\eta\leq\bar{\eta},\quad\text{and}\quad 0<\hat{\eta}\leq\bar{\hat{\eta}},\end{array}

(86)

where

\begin{array}[]{lll}\bar{\eta}:=\min\Big\{\frac{1}{4L_{w}\sqrt{2\Lambda_{0}(\Theta_{w}+1)}},\frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}},\frac{1}{2L_{w}},\frac{1}{2L_{u}\kappa}\Big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{\hat{\eta}}:=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{H}}}{2\sqrt{14\Lambda_{0}L_{u}^{3}(\Theta_{u}+1)}}.\end{array}

(87)

Then, the following bounds hold:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{4[2\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-2\Psi_{0}^{\star}+L_{u}^{2}\eta\|\widetilde{u}^{0}-u^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{0})\|^{2}]}{\eta(T+1)}+8(C_{w}+C_{u})\eta^{2}.\end{array}

(88)

For a given
\epsilon>0
, if we choose both
\eta:=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
and
\hat{\eta}:=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
satisfying (
83
) and
T:=\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\big)
, then
\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}
.

Consequently, Algorithm
2
requires
\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{n}{\epsilon^{4}}\big)
evaluations of
\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}_{i}}
and
\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{n}{\epsilon^{3}}\big)
evaluations of
\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
to achieve an
\epsilon
-stationary point
\widehat{w}_{T}
of (
1
) computed by (
19
).
This algorithm also requires
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
evaluations of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
and
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-4})
evaluations of
\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}
.

Proof of Theorem
8
.

Since
\mu_{H}>0
and
\mu_{h}=0
, for
C_{S}
given in Lemma
13
, it reduces to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}C_{S}&:=&\big[\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{j}\big]\cdot\big[\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{s}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{ns}\big]\\
&=&\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{ns+j}\\
&\leq&\frac{n}{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}.\end{array}

In this case, we can lower bound
B_{t}
from Lemma
17
as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}B_{t}&:=&1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}-2L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{2L_{u}^{3}}{n}(1+\omega+2\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2})\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}\cdot C_{S}\cdot\Lambda_{0}(\Theta_{u}+1)\\
&\geq&1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}-2L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{2}-\frac{2L_{u}^{3}}{\mu_{H}}(1+\omega+2\omega^{2}L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2})\Lambda_{0}(\Theta_{u}+1)\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}.\end{array}

Since
\eta_{t}:=\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}]
for
\bar{\eta}
satisfying (
87
), we have
\eta\leq\frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}}
,
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2L_{w}}
, and
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2L_{u}\kappa}
.
Moreover, we choose
\omega:=1
and
\hat{\eta}_{t}:=\hat{\eta}\in(0,\bar{\hat{\eta}}]
.
Hence, we can further lower bound
B_{t}
as
