\begin{array}[]{lcl}B_{t}&\geq&\frac{1}{2}-2\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta^{2}-\frac{2\Lambda_{0}L_{u}^{3}(1+\omega+\omega M_{\omega}\eta^{2})}{\mu_{H}}(\Theta_{u}+1)\hat{\eta}^{2}\\
&=&\frac{1}{2}-2\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta^{2}-\frac{2\Lambda_{0}L_{u}^{3}(2+M_{1}\eta^{2})}{\mu_{H}}(\Theta_{u}+1)\hat{\eta}^{2},\end{array}

(89)

where
M_{\omega}:=2\omega L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+\frac{4L_{w}^{2}}{\omega}=M_{1}=2L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+4L_{w}^{2}
.

We can see that the second condition of (
83
) holds if

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}}{n}\big)^{nS}\big(2+M_{1}\eta^{2}\big)\leq 1,\\
\Leftrightarrow&-nS\ln\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}}{n}\big)\geq\ln\big(2+M_{1}\eta^{2}\big).\end{array}

Since
\eta\leq\frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}}
and
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2L_{w}}
, we have
M_{1}\eta^{2}=(2L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+4L_{w}^{2})\eta^{2}\leq\frac{3}{2}
.
Using this relation, and
-\ln(1-\tau)\geq\tau
for
\tau\in(0,1)
, the last condition holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}S\geq\ln(7/2)\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad S\geq\frac{\ln(7/2)}{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}}.\end{array}

Hence, we can choose
S:=\lfloor\frac{\ln(7/2)}{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}}\rfloor
as stated in (
86
).

The condition (
89
) holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}B_{t}&\geq&\frac{1}{2}-2\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta^{2}-\frac{7\Lambda_{0}L_{u}^{3}(\Theta_{u}+1)}{\mu_{H}}\hat{\eta}^{2}.\end{array}

This condition shows that if we choose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}0<\eta\leq\frac{1}{4L_{w}\sqrt{2\Lambda_{0}(\Theta_{w}+1)}}\quad\text{and}\quad 0<\hat{\eta}\leq\bar{\hat{\eta}}:=\frac{\sqrt{\mu_{H}}}{2\sqrt{14\Lambda_{0}L_{u}^{3}(\Theta_{u}+1)}},\end{array}

then, from (
89
), we have
B_{t}\geq\frac{1}{4}
.
Due to (
86
), both conditions here are satisfied.

Next, let us define
C_{w}
and
C_{u}
as in (
85
), respectively, i.e.:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}C_{w}&:=&L_{w}^{2}\big[(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{1}+3\sigma_{w}^{2}\big],\quad\text{and}\quad C_{u}:=\frac{7L_{u}^{3}}{\mu_{H}}\big[\Lambda_{1}(\Theta_{u}+1)+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big].\end{array}

In this case, (
84
) reduces to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})+\frac{L_{u}^{2}\eta}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}&\leq&\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\frac{L_{u}^{2}\eta}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta}{8}\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+C_{w}\eta^{3}+C_{u}\hat{\eta}^{2}\eta.\end{array}

Subtracting
\Psi_{0}^{\star}
from both sides of this inequality, and averaging the result from
t=0
to
T
, and noting that
\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{T})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}\geq 0
, we obtain (
88
).

To achieve an
\epsilon
-stationary point of (
3
), from (
88
), we need to impose the following condition:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{2[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}]}{\eta(T+1)}+\frac{L_{u}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}^{0}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{0})\|^{2}}{T+1}+2C_{w}\eta^{2}+2C_{u}\hat{\eta}^{2}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{4}.\end{array}

Since other terms are constant, if we choose
\eta:=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
and
\hat{\eta}:=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
such that they still satisfies (
86
), then we can choose
T:=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
to guarantee the last condition.
Since each iteration
t
, we run
S
epochs of the shuffling scheme (
27
), the total number of evaluations of
\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
is
\mathcal{T}_{u}:=T\times S\times n
.
However, we have
S=\lfloor\frac{\ln(7/2)}{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}}\rfloor=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-1})
, we get
\mathcal{T}_{u}:=\mathcal{O}(n\epsilon^{-4})
.
The total number of evaluations of
\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
is
\mathcal{T}_{w}:=Tn=\mathcal{O}(n\epsilon^{-3})
as stated.

Finally, since each epoch
t
, Algorithm
2
requires one evaluation of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
, and
S
evaluations of
\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}
, but since
S=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-1})
, the total number of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
evaluations is
T=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
, while the total number of
\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}
evaluations is
TS=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-4})
.
Overall, Algorithm
2
needs
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
evaluations of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
and
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-4})
evaluations of
\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Since
\mu_{h}>0
and
\mu_{H}=0
, for
C_{S}
given by Lemma
13
, it reduces to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}C_{S}&:=&\big[\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{j}\big]\cdot\big[\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{s}}\big(1-\frac{\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\big)^{ns}\big]\\
&=&\sum_{s=0}^{S-1}\frac{1}{(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})^{s+1}}\leq\frac{1}{2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}.\end{array}

In this case, we can lower bound
B_{t}
from Lemma
17
as in Theorem
3
, i.e.:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}B_{t}&\geq&1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t}-2L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\Lambda_{0}\eta_{t}^{2}-\frac{2L_{u}^{3}}{n\mu_{H}}(1+L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+2L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2})\Lambda_{0}(\Theta_{u}+1)\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}.\end{array}

We need to choose
\eta
as in Theorem
3
.
Since
\eta_{t}=\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}]
, we have
\eta\leq\frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}}
,
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2L_{w}}
, and
\eta\leq\frac{1}{2L_{u}\kappa}
.
Alternatively, we also have
\hat{\eta}_{t}:=\hat{\eta}\in(0,\bar{\hat{\eta}}]
.
Therefore, we can further lower bound
B_{t}
as
B_{t}\geq\frac{1}{4}
as in Theorem
3
.

Now, the second condition of (
83
) holds if

\begin{array}[]{lcl}(1+2\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}\big)^{S}\geq 2+(2L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+4L_{w}^{2})\eta^{2}.\end{array}

Using the fact that
\ln(1+e)\geq\frac{e}{2}
for
e\in(0,1/2)
and
(2L_{u}^{2}\kappa^{2}+4L_{w}^{2})\eta^{2}\leq\frac{3}{2}
, the last inequality holds if
\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}S\geq\ln(7/2)
.
Hence, we can choose
S:=\big\lfloor\frac{\ln(7/2)}{\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}}\big\rfloor
as stated in Theorem
4
.
The remaining proof follows from Theorem
3
.
∎

C.4
Convergence of the full-shuffling variant of Algorithm
2
– The case
S=1

In this subsection, we analyze the convergence of Algorithm
2
using only one epoch of the
shuffling gradient ascent
scheme (
27
).
In this case, by dropping the superscript
s
, the scheme (
27
) can be simplified as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}&u_{0}^{(t)}:=\widetilde{u}_{t-1},\\
&\textrm{For $i=1,2,\cdots,n$, update}\\
&\qquad u_{i}^{(t)}:=u_{i-1}^{(t)}+\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}_{\pi^{(t)}(i)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{i-1}^{(t)}),\\
&\widetilde{u}_{t}:=\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}(u_{n}^{(t)}),\end{array}\right.

(90)

where
\hat{\eta}_{t}>0
is a given learning rate.

We divide our analysis into different tasks as follows.

C.4.1
Potential function and a technical lemma

One key step of our analysis is to construct an appropriate potential function.
We exploit the ideas from
[
9
]
to construct this function as follows.

For
\Psi_{0}
defined by (
3
) and
\mathcal{L}
given in (
1
), we consider the following
potential function
:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(w,u):=\lambda\big[\Psi_{0}(w)-\Psi_{0}^{\star}\big]+\Psi_{0}(w)-\mathcal{L}(w,u),\end{array}

(91)

where
\lambda>0
is a given parameter determined later.
Since
\Psi_{0}(w)\geq\Psi_{0}^{\star}:=\inf_{w}\Psi_{0}(w)
and
\Psi_{0}(w)=\sup_{u}\mathcal{L}(w,u)\geq\mathcal{L}(w,u)
, it is obvious to see that
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(w,u)\geq 0
for all
(w,u)\in\mathrm{dom}\left(\mathcal{L}\right)
.

Similar to (
18
), we consider the following gradient mappings for both (
2
) and (
3
), respectively:

\begin{array}[]{lcllcl}\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1}):=\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}\big(\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}\big),&\text{where}&\quad\widehat{u}_{t}:=\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}\big(\widetilde{u}_{t-1}+\hat{\eta}_{t}\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\big),\\
\mathcal{G}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}):=\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}\big(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-\widehat{w}_{t}\big),&\text{where}&\quad\widehat{w}_{t}:=\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}\big(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-\eta_{t}\nabla{\Phi}_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\big).\end{array}

(92)

We need the following result.

Lemma 18
.

Let
\widehat{u}_{t}
and
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})
be defined by (
92
), and
\psi
be defined by (
56
).
Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}2\mu_{\psi}\|\widehat{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})-\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})).\end{array}

(93)

If
0<\hat{\eta}_{t}\leq\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}
, then we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}2\mu_{\psi}[\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})-\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))]\leq\left(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\right)\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(94)

Proof.

Since
\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\cdot)
is
\mu_{\psi}
-strongly convex and
u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}):=\mathrm{arg}\!\displaystyle\min_{u}\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u)
, for
\widehat{u}_{t}
given in (
92
), we easily obtain (
93
).

Next, using again the
\mu_{\psi}
-strong convexity of
\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\cdot)
and
u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}):=\mathrm{arg}\!\displaystyle\min_{u}\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u)
, for
\widehat{u}_{t}
given in (
92
), by
[
31
, Theorem 2.1.10]
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}2\mu_{\psi}[\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})-\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))]\leq\|\nabla_{u}{\psi}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(95)

where
\nabla_{u}{\psi}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})=-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})+\nabla{h}(\widehat{u}_{t})\in\partial{\psi}(\widehat{w}_{t}):=-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})+\partial{h}(\widehat{u}_{t})
.

Now, for
\widehat{u}_{t}
defined by (
92
), we have
\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t})+\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\in\partial{h}(\widehat{u}_{t})
, leading to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\nabla_{u}{\psi}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t}):=\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t})+\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})\in\partial{\psi}(\widehat{u}_{t}).\end{array}

(96)

Since
-\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\cdot)
is
L_{u}
-smooth and
\mu_{H}
-strongly convex, by
[
31
, Theorem 2.1.12]
, we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}&-\langle\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t}),\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}\rangle\geq\frac{L_{u}\mu_{H}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}\|^{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad+{~}\frac{1}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\|\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(97)

Utilizing (
96
) and (
97
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\nabla_{u}{\psi}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})\|^{2}&=&\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{2}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}\langle\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t}),\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}\rangle\\
&&+{~}\|\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\right)\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\left(\frac{2}{\hat{\eta}_{t}(L_{u}+\mu_{H})}-1\right)\|\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\nabla_{u}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})\|^{2}.\end{array}

Substituting this inequality into (
95
) and noting that
0<\hat{\eta}_{t}\leq\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}
and
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1}):=\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}\big(\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}\big)
, we obtain (
94
).
∎

C.4.2
A key bound for the shuffling gradient descent scheme (
28
)

The following lemma bounds the difference
\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})
.

Lemma 19
.

Suppose that Assumptions
4
and
7
hold.
Let
\mathcal{L}
be defined by (
1
) and
g_{t}
be defined by (
68
).
Then, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})&\leq&\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})\|^{2}+\frac{3+(L_{f}+L_{w})\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(98)

Proof.

From (
68
) and (
69
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}g_{t}&:=&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{\hat{\pi}^{(t)}(j)}(w_{j-1}^{(t)},\widetilde{u}_{t})\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM2_w_update_upto_i}}{=}\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-w_{n}^{(t)})=\frac{1}{\eta_{t}}(w_{0}^{(t)}-w_{n}^{(t)}).\end{array}

Since
\widetilde{w}_{t}=\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}(w_{n}^{(t)})
from the second line of (
28
), we have
f^{\prime}(\widetilde{w}_{t}):=\eta_{t}^{-1}\big(w_{n}^{(t)}-\widetilde{w}_{t}\big)=-g_{t}-\eta_{t}^{-1}(\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\in\partial{f}(\widetilde{w}_{t})
.
Hence, by (
30
) from Assumption
7
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}f(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})&\leq&f(\widetilde{w}_{t})+\langle f^{\prime}(\widetilde{w}_{t}),\widetilde{w}_{t-1}-\widetilde{w}_{t}\rangle+\frac{L_{f}}{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&=&f(\widetilde{w}_{t})+\langle g_{t},\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\rangle+\frac{2+L_{f}\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Next, by the
L
-smoothness of
\mathcal{H}
from (
12
) of Assumption
4
, we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})&\leq&\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\langle\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t}),\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\rangle+\frac{L_{w}}{2}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Summing up the last two inequalities and using
\mathcal{L}(w,\widetilde{u}_{t})=f(w)+\mathcal{H}(w,\widetilde{u}_{t})-h(\widetilde{u}_{t})
from (
1
) and Young’s inequality in
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
, we can show that

\hskip 0.0pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})&\leq&\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})+\langle g_{t}-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t}),\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\rangle+\frac{2+(L_{f}+L_{w})\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\leq}&\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})\|^{2}+\frac{3+(L_{f}+L_{w})\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2},\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

which proves (
98
).
∎

C.4.3
Key bounds for the shuffling gradient ascent scheme (
27
)

We also derive necessary bounds to analyze Algorithm
2
using the simplified version (
90
) of the
shuffling gradient ascent
scheme (
27
).
Let us define

\begin{array}[]{lcl}v_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{j-1}^{(t)}).\end{array}

(99)

We establish the following two lemmas.

Lemma 20
.

Suppose that Assumption
4
holds for (
1
).
Let
\{u_{i}^{(t)}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be generated by the simplified version (
90
) of (
27
).
Then, if we choose
\hat{\eta}_{t}>0
such that
1-3L_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\geq 0
, then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\Delta}_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\|u_{i}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}&\leq&(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\cdot\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}+3\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\sigma_{u}^{2}.\end{array}

(100)

Let
v_{t}
be defined by (
99
).
Then, we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|v_{t}-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{L_{u}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|u_{i-1}^{(t)}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}=L_{u}^{2}\hat{\Delta}_{t}.\end{array}

(101)

Proof.

First, for simplicity of notation, we denote
\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}:=\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})
.
Then, from the update of
u_{i}^{(t)}
in (
90
), for any
i\in[n]
, we have

u_{i}^{(t)}=u_{0}^{(t)}+\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{j-1}^{(t)}).

(102)

Using this expression and Young’s inequality in
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
and
{\tiny\textcircled{2}}
below, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|u_{i}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:ut_update_upto_i}}{=}&\frac{i^{2}\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{j-1}^{(t)})\|^{2}\\
&\overset{{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\leq}&\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\big\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big[\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{(t)})-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}\big]\big\|^{2}+\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\big\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big[\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{j-1}^{(t)})-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{(t)})\big]\big\|^{2}\\
&\overset{{\tiny\textcircled{2}}}{\leq}&\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\big\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big[\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{(t)})-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}\big]\big\|^{2}+\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{3i\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{j-1}^{(t)})-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{(t)})\big\|^{2}.\end{array}

Now, we denote
\hat{\Delta}_{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\|u_{j}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\|u_{j}^{(t)}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}
.
Then, by (
12
) from Assumption
4
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{j-1}^{(t)})-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{(t)})\big\|^{2}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:Lsmooth2}}{\leq}&\frac{L_{u}^{2}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\|u^{(t)}_{j-1}-u_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&L_{u}^{2}\hat{\Delta}_{t}.\end{array}

Next, by Young’s inequality again in
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
,
u_{0}^{(t)}=\widetilde{u}_{t-1}
, and (
14
) from Assumption
4
, and the fact that
\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}:=\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})
, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{T}_{[2]}&:=&\|\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big[\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{(t)})-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{t-1}\big]\big\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\textcircled{1}}{\leq}&\frac{1}{i}\sum_{j=1}^{i}\big\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1}))\big\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:bounded_variance2b}}{\leq}&\frac{n}{i}\big[\Theta_{u}\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big].\end{array}

Combining the last three inequalities above, we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|u_{i}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{3i\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n}\big[\Theta_{u}\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\sigma_{u}^{2}\big]\\
&&+{~}\frac{3i^{2}\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{3i\cdot L_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n}\hat{\Delta}_{t}\\
&=&\frac{3i\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}(n\Theta_{u}+i)\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{3i\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n}\sigma_{u}^{2}+\frac{3i\cdot L_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n}\hat{\Delta}_{t}.\end{array}

Averaging this inequality from
i=0
to
i=n-1
, we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\hat{\Delta}_{t}&:=&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\|u_{i}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\big[\frac{3i\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n^{2}}(n\Theta_{u}+i)\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{3i\cdot\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n}\sigma_{u}^{2}+\frac{3i\cdot L_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{n}\hat{\Delta}_{t}\big]\\
&\leq&\frac{(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{2}\cdot\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{3\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{2}\sigma_{u}^{2}+\frac{3L_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{2}\hat{\Delta}_{t}.\end{array}

Here, we have used the facts that
\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}i=\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\leq\frac{n^{2}}{2}
and
\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}i^{2}=\frac{n(n-1)(2n-1)}{6}\leq\frac{n^{3}}{3}
.
Rearranging the last inequality and noting that
1-\frac{3L_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, we obtain (
100
).

Finally, using (
99
) and (
12
) from Assumption
4
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|v_{t}-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM3_vt_quantity}}{=}&\big\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big[\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(i)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{i-1}^{(t)})-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(i)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\big]\big\|^{2}\\
&\leq&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(i)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{i-1}^{(t)})-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(i)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\big\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:Lsmooth2}}{\leq}&\frac{L_{u}^{2}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|u_{i-1}^{(t)}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2},\end{array}

which proves (
101
).
∎

Lemma 21
.

Suppose that Assumption
4
holds for (
1
).
Let
\{u_{i}^{(t)}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be generated by the simplified version (
90
) of (
27
),
\psi
be defined by (
56
),
v_{t}
be defined by (
99
), and
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}}
be defined as in (
92
).
Then

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})&\leq&\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}[1+(\mu_{h}+\mu_{H})\hat{\eta}_{t}-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t}]}{2}\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{(1-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}+\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\|v_{t}-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(103)

Proof.

Let us denote
\widehat{u}_{t}:=\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}\big(\widetilde{u}_{t-1}+\hat{\eta}_{t}\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\big)
as in (
92
).
Then, from (
92
), we have
\hat{\eta}_{t}{\hat{\mathcal{G}}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})=\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}
.
Moreover, we can show that
\nabla{h}(\widehat{u}_{t}):=\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}\big(\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}\big)+\nabla{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\in\partial{h}(\widehat{u}_{t})
.

By the
\mu_{h}
-strong convexity of
h
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}h(\widehat{u}_{t})&\leq&h(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})+\langle\nabla{h}(\widehat{u}_{t}),\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\rangle-\frac{\mu_{h}}{2}\|\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&=&h(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})+\langle\nabla{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1}),\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\rangle-\frac{2+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Next, by the
L_{u}
-smoothness of
\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\cdot)
from Assumption
4
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}-\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})&\leq&-\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\langle\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1}),\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\rangle+\frac{L_{u}}{2}\|\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Summing up the last two inequalities and using both
\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u):=-\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u)+h(u)
from (
56
) and
\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}=-\hat{\eta}_{t}{\hat{\mathcal{G}}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})
, we can show that

\hskip 0.0pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})&\leq&\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\frac{(2+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&=&\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}(2+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2}\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(104)

Next, from (
99
) and (
90
), one can derive that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}v_{t}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM3_vt_quantity}}{:=}&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{\pi^{(t)}(j)}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{j-1}^{(t)})\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM3_SGM4lower_prob}}{=}\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(u_{n}^{(t)}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1})=\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(u_{n}^{(t)}-u_{0}^{(t)}).\end{array}

(105)

Since
\widetilde{u}_{t}=\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}(u_{n}^{(t)})
from (
90
), we have
\nabla{h}(\widetilde{u}_{t}):=\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}\big(u_{n}^{(t)}-\widetilde{u}_{t}\big)=v_{t}-\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\in\partial{h}(\widetilde{u}_{t})
.
Hence, again by the
\mu_{h}
-strong convexity of
h
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}h(\widetilde{u}_{t})&\leq&h(\widehat{u}_{t})+\langle\nabla{h}(\widetilde{u}_{t}),\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\rangle-\frac{\mu_{h}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\|^{2}\\
&=&h(\widehat{u}_{t})+\langle v_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\rangle-\frac{1}{\hat{\eta}_{t}}\langle\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\rangle-\frac{\mu_{h}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\|^{2}\\
&=&h(\widehat{u}_{t})+\langle v_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\rangle-\frac{1}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Again, by the
L_{u}
-smoothness and
\mu_{H}
-strong concavity of
\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\cdot)
from Assumption
4
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}-\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})&\leq&-\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\langle\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1}),\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\rangle+\frac{L_{u}}{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2},\\
-\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})&\leq&-\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})-\langle\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1}),\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}\rangle-\frac{\mu_{H}}{2}\|\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

Adding the last three inequalities together, and using
\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u)=h(u)-\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u)
from (
56
) and
\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}=-\hat{\eta}_{t}\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})
, we have

\hskip 0.0pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})&\leq&\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})-\langle\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-v_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\rangle-\frac{(1-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{1-\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widehat{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\|^{2}\\
&\overset{{\tiny\textcircled{1}}}{\leq}&\varphi_{t}(\widehat{u}_{t})+\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-v_{t}\|^{2}-\frac{(1-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}(1-\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2}\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2},\end{array}\hskip-8.61108pt

(106)

where we have used Young’s inequality in the last line
{\tiny\textcircled{1}}
as
\langle\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-v_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\rangle\leq\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-v_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widehat{u}_{t}\|^{2}
.

Finally, summing up (
104
) and (
106
), we get

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})&\leq&\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\frac{(1-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}[1+(\mu_{h}+\mu_{H})\hat{\eta}_{t}-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t}]}{2}\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\|v_{t}-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2},\end{array}

which proves (
103
).
∎

C.4.4
Convergence analysis of the full-shuffling variant of Algorithm
2
– The case
S=1

To analyze the convergence of the full-shuffling variant of Algorithm
2
, we need the following lemma.

Lemma 22
.

Let
\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}
be defined by (
91
),
\mathcal{V}_{t}:=\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})
, and
g_{t}
and
v_{t}
be defined by (
68
) and (
99
), respectively.
Suppose that
f
satisfies (
30
) of Assumption
7
.
Then, the following bound holds:

\hskip-1.07639pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\mathcal{V}_{t-1}&\leq&-\frac{\lambda-2-[(1+\lambda)L_{\Phi_{0}}+L_{w}+L_{f}]\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{(1-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{(\lambda+1)\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}(1+(\mu_{h}+\mu_{H})\hat{\eta}_{t}-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2}\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{(\lambda+1)\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\|v_{t}-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-17.22217pt

(107)

Proof.

From (
91
), if we denote
\mathcal{V}_{t}:=\mathcal{V}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})
, then we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\mathcal{V}_{t-1}&=&(\lambda+1)\big[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})-\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\big]+\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})\\
&=&(\lambda+1)\big[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})-\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\big]+\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})-\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})\\
&&+{~}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})\\
&=&(\lambda+1)\big[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})-\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\big]+\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})\\
&&+{~}\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1}).\end{array}

(108)

Next, from (
72
), we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t})-\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})&\leq&-\frac{\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\frac{(1-L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(109)

From (
98
), we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})&\leq&\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})\|^{2}+\frac{3+(L_{f}+L_{w})\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(110)

From (
103
), we can rewrite it as

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})-\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})&\leq&-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}[1+(\mu_{h}+\mu_{H})\hat{\eta}_{t}-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t}]}{2}\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\|v_{t}-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{(1-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}.\end{array}

(111)

Substituting (
109
), (
110
), and (
111
) into (
108
), we can derive that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\mathcal{V}_{t-1}&\leq&-\frac{\lambda-2-[(1+\lambda)L_{\Phi_{0}}+L_{w}+L_{f}]\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{(1-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{(\lambda+1)\eta_{t}(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}-\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}(1+(\mu_{h}+\mu_{H})\hat{\eta}_{t}-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2}\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{(\lambda+1)\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\|g_{t}-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}\|v_{t}-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2},\end{array}

which proves (
107
).
∎

Next, we further upper bound (
107
) from Lemma
22
as follows.

Lemma 23
.

Under the same condition as in Lemma
22
,
1-3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\geq 0
,
1-3L_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\geq 0
, and
\hat{\eta}_{t}\leq\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\mathcal{V}_{t-1}&\leq&-\frac{C_{0}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{1-(L_{u}+3C_{1})\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{C_{3}\eta_{t}}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\big(\mu_{\psi}C_{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}-\frac{3C_{1}\eta_{t}}{4\mu_{\psi}}\big)\big[\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})-\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))\big]+C_{4}\eta_{t}^{3}+C_{5}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3},\end{array}

(112)

where
C_{i}
for
i=0,1,\cdots,5
are respectively given as follows:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}C_{0}&:=&\lambda-2-[(1+\lambda)L_{\Phi_{0}}+L_{w}+L_{f}]\eta_{t},\\
C_{1}&:=&L_{u}^{2}[\lambda+1+4(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}],\\
C_{2}&:=&1-(L_{u}-\mu_{\psi})\hat{\eta}_{t}-\frac{\hat{\Lambda}_{0}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}-3C_{1}\eta_{t}\hat{\eta}_{t},\\
C_{3}&:=&(\lambda+1)(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})-2\Lambda_{0}(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{2},\\
C_{4}&:=&(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}\big[3\sigma_{w}^{2}+\Lambda_{1}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\big],\\
C_{5}&:=&\frac{\hat{\Lambda}_{1}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)+3L_{u}^{2}\sigma_{u}^{2}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}.\end{array}\right.

(113)

Proof.

First, since
1-3L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\geq 0
, combining the last line of (
71
) and (
70
) of Lemma
14
, we can show that

\hskip-1.07639pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g_{t}-\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM2_key_bounds_for_gt_2}}{\leq}&\frac{L_{w}^{2}}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|w_{i-1}^{(t)}-w_{0}^{(t)}\|^{2}+L_{u}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM2_key_bounds_for_wt_2}}{\leq}&L_{u}^{2}\big(4L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+1\big)\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+6L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\\
&&+{~}2L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{2}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}\hskip-12.91663pt

(114)

Similarly, from the first line of (
71
) and (
70
), we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|g_{t}-\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t})\|^{2}&\leq&4L_{w}^{2}L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+6L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\\
&&+{~}2L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{2}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}.\end{array}

(115)

Next, since
1-3L_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}\geq 0
, combining (
100
) and (
101
) of Lemma
20
, and (
29
) from Assumption
6
, we have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|v_{t}-\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\|\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}+3L_{u}^{2}\sigma_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\\
&\leq&\hat{\Lambda}_{0}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\big(\hat{\Lambda}_{1}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)+3L_{u}^{2}\sigma_{u}^{2}\big)\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}.\end{array}

(116)

Substituting (
114
), (
115
), and (
116
) into (
107
), and noting that
\mu_{\psi}:=\mu_{h}+\mu_{H}>0
, we obtain

\hskip-1.07639pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\mathcal{V}_{t-1}&\leq&-\frac{\lambda-2-[(1+\lambda)L_{\Phi_{0}}+L_{w}+L_{f}]\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{(1-L_{u}\hat{\eta}_{t})}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{(\lambda+1)(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})\eta_{t}}{2}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{3}\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2}\big[1-(L_{u}-\mu_{\psi})\hat{\eta}_{t}-\frac{\hat{\Lambda}_{0}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}\big]\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{L_{u}^{2}\eta_{t}}{2}\big[4(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+\lambda+1\big]\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}3(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{3}+\frac{[\hat{\Lambda}_{1}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)+3L_{u}^{2}\sigma_{u}^{2}]\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})}.\end{array}\hskip-17.22217pt

(117)

Next, by Young’s inequality, (
92
), and (
93
), we can show that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}&\leq&3\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}+3\|\widetilde{u}_{t-1}-\widehat{u}_{t}\|^{2}+3\|\widehat{u}_{t}-u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&\overset{\tiny\eqref{eq:SGM3_gradient_mappings},\eqref{eq:SGM3_key_properties_of_psi_p1}}{\leq}&3\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}+3\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&+{~}\frac{3}{2\mu_{\psi}}\big[\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})-\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))\big].\end{array}

Substituting the last inequality and
\|\nabla{\Phi_{0}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\leq\Lambda_{0}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\Lambda_{1}
from (
20
) of Assumption
5
into (
117
), we can derive that

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\mathcal{V}_{t-1}&\leq&-\frac{\lambda-2-[(1+\lambda)L_{\Phi_{0}}+L_{w}+L_{f}]\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{1-(L_{u}+3C_{1}\eta_{t})\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\big[(\lambda+1)(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})-2\Lambda_{0}(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{2}\big]\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{C_{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2}\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}+\frac{3C_{1}\eta_{t}}{4\mu_{\psi}}\big[\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})-\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))\big]\\
&&+{~}\big[3(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}\sigma_{w}^{2}+\Lambda_{1}(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\big]\eta_{t}^{3}\\
&&+{~}\frac{\big[\hat{\Lambda}_{1}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)+3L_{u}^{2}\sigma_{u}^{2}\big]\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})},\end{array}

(118)

where
C_{1}:=L_{u}^{2}\big[4(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}+\lambda+1\big]
and
C_{2}:=1-(L_{u}-\mu_{h}-\mu_{H})\hat{\eta}_{t}-\frac{\hat{\Lambda}_{0}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}_{t}^{2}}{1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t}}-3C_{1}\eta_{t}\hat{\eta}_{t}
.

Finally, from (
94
), since
\hat{\eta}_{t}\leq\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}
, we also have

\begin{array}[]{lcl}-\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\leq-\left(1-\frac{2L_{u}\mu_{H}\hat{\eta}_{t}}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}\right)\|\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}}(\widetilde{u}_{t-1})\|^{2}\leq-2\mu_{\psi}[\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})-\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))].\end{array}

Substituting this inequality into (
118
), we arrive at

\hskip-1.07639pt\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{t}-\mathcal{V}_{t-1}&\leq&-\frac{\lambda-2-[(1+\lambda)L_{\Phi_{0}}+L_{w}+L_{f}]\eta_{t}}{2\eta_{t}}\|\widetilde{w}_{t}-\widetilde{w}_{t-1}\|^{2}-\frac{1-(L_{u}+3C_{1}\eta_{t})\hat{\eta}_{t}}{2\hat{\eta}_{t}}\|\widetilde{u}_{t}-\widetilde{u}_{t-1}\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\frac{\eta_{t}}{2}\big[(\lambda+1)(1-2L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta_{t})-2\Lambda_{0}(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta_{t}^{2}\big]\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}\\
&&-{~}\big(\mu_{\psi}C_{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}-\frac{3C_{1}\eta_{t}}{4\mu_{\psi}}\big)\big[\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},\widehat{u}_{t})-\psi(\widetilde{w}_{t-1},u_{0}^{*}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1}))\big]\\
&&+{~}(\lambda+2)L_{w}^{2}\big[3\sigma_{w}^{2}+\Lambda_{1}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\big]\eta_{t}^{3}+\frac{\big[\hat{\Lambda}_{1}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)+3L_{u}^{2}\sigma_{u}^{2}\big]\hat{\eta}_{t}^{3}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}_{t})},\end{array}\hskip-17.22217pt

which is exactly (
112
).
∎

Now, we are ready to prove the convergence of Algorithm
2
using only
one epoch
(i.e.
S=1
) of the
shuffling routine (
27
)
.
The following theorem is the full version of Theorem
5
in the main text.

Theorem 9
.

Suppose that Assumptions
1
,
2
,
4
,
5
,
6
, and
7
hold for (
1
) under the
(
NC
)
setting.
Let
\Psi_{0}
be defined by (
3
), and
{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}
be defined by (
18
).
Let us denote
C_{w}
and
C_{u}
respectively by

\begin{array}[]{lcl}C_{w}&:=&5L_{w}^{2}\big[\Lambda_{1}(3\Theta_{w}+1)+3\sigma_{w}^{2}\big]\quad\text{and}\quad C_{u}:=\frac{L_{u}^{2}}{2}\big[\hat{\Lambda}_{1}(3\Theta_{u}+2)+3\sigma_{u}^{2}\big].\end{array}

(119)

Let
\{(\widetilde{w}_{t},\widetilde{u}_{t})\}
be generated by Algorithm
2
using only
one epoch

(
i.e.
S=1
)
of the
shuffling routine (
27
)
, and fixed learning rates
\eta_{t}:=\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}]
and
\hat{\eta}_{t}:=\hat{\eta}:=15\kappa^{2}\eta
, where

\begin{array}[]{lll}\bar{\eta}:=\min\Big\{\frac{1}{60\kappa^{2}L_{u}},\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{10\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)}},\ \frac{2\sqrt{L_{u}}}{\kappa\sqrt{15(4L_{u}^{2}+\mu_{\psi}^{2})}},\ \frac{\sqrt{L_{u}}}{15\kappa\sqrt{2L_{u}^{3}\hat{\Lambda}_{0}(3\Theta_{u}+2)+\mu_{\psi}^{2}}},\ \frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}+L_{w}+L_{f}}\Big\}.\end{array}

Then, the following bound holds:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}&\leq&\frac{24[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}]+8[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{0},\widetilde{u}_{0})]}{\eta(T+1)}+8C_{w}\eta^{2}+\frac{8C_{u}\hat{\eta}^{3}}{\eta}\end{array}

(120)

For a given
\epsilon>0
, if
\eta:=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\in(0,\bar{\eta}]
and
T:=\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\big)
, then
\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}
.

Consequently, Algorithm
2
requires
\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{n}{\epsilon^{3}}\big)
evaluations of both
\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}_{i}}
and
\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
, and
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
evaluations of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
and
\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}
to achieve an
\epsilon
-stationary point
\widehat{w}_{T}
of (
1
) computed by (
19
).

Proof.

Let us choose
\lambda:=3
,
\eta_{t}:=\eta>0
and
\hat{\eta}_{t}:=\hat{\eta}>0
.
First, we need to guarantee that
1-3L_{w}^{2}\eta^{2}\geq 0
and
1-3L_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}_{t}\geq 0
in Theorem
23
.
Suppose that
\eta\leq\frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}+L_{w}+L_{f}}
.
Then, since
L_{\Phi_{0}}=(1+\kappa)L_{w}\geq L_{w}
, we have
\eta\leq\frac{1}{5L_{w}}
, which obviously guarantees that
1-3L_{w}^{2}\eta^{2}\geq 0
.

Moreover, for
C_{i}
for
i=0,\cdots,5
defined by (
113
), we can show that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}C_{0}&:=&1-(4L_{\Phi_{0}}+L_{w}+L_{f})\eta\geq 0,\\
C_{1}&:=&L_{u}^{2}(4+20L_{w}^{2}\eta^{2})\leq 5L_{u}^{2},\\
C_{2}&:=&1-(L_{u}-\mu_{\psi})\hat{\eta}-\frac{\hat{\Lambda}_{0}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}^{2}}{1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta}}-6C_{1}\eta\hat{\eta}^{2}\geq 1-L_{u}\hat{\eta}-\hat{\Lambda}_{0}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}^{2}-30L_{u}^{2}\eta\hat{\eta}^{2},\\
C_{3}&:=&4-8L_{\Phi_{0}}\eta-10\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta^{2}\geq 2\big[1-5\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta^{2}\big],\\
C_{4}&:=&5L_{w}^{2}\big[\Lambda_{1}(3\Theta_{w}+1)+3\sigma_{w}^{2}\big]=C_{w},\\
C_{5}&:=&\frac{\hat{\Lambda}_{1}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)+3L_{u}^{2}\sigma_{u}^{2}}{2(1+\mu_{h}\hat{\eta})}\leq C_{u}.\end{array}\right.

Now, suppose that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}&L_{u}\hat{\eta}\leq\frac{1}{4},\quad\hat{\Lambda}_{0}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}^{2}+30L_{u}^{2}\eta\hat{\eta}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2},\quad(L_{u}+15L_{u}^{2}\eta)\hat{\eta}\leq 1,\\
&4C_{2}\mu_{\psi}^{2}\hat{\eta}\geq 3C_{1}\eta,\quad\text{and}\quad 5\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2},\end{array}\right.

(121)

then we can easily show that
C_{2}\geq\frac{1}{4}
,
C_{3}\geq 1
,
1-(L_{u}+3C_{1}\eta)\hat{\eta}\geq 0
, and
\mu_{\psi}C_{2}\hat{\eta}-\frac{3C_{1}\eta}{4\mu_{\psi}}\geq 0
.

In this case, (
112
) reduces to

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\mathcal{V}_{t}&\leq&\mathcal{V}_{t-1}-\frac{\eta}{8}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta_{t}}(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})\|^{2}+C_{w}\eta^{3}+C_{u}\hat{\eta}^{3}.\end{array}

(122)

By induction, we obtain (
120
) from (
122
) and
\mathcal{V}_{0}:=3[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi^{\star}_{0}]+\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{0},\widetilde{u}_{0})
.

From (
121
), let us choose
\hat{\eta}=\frac{15L_{u}^{2}}{\mu_{\psi}^{2}}\eta=15\kappa^{2}\eta
with
\kappa:=\frac{L_{u}}{\mu_{\psi}}
.
Then, we can verify the five conditions of (
121
) as follows.

•

We have
4C_{2}\mu_{\psi}^{2}\hat{\eta}\geq\mu_{\psi}^{2}\hat{\eta}=15L_{u}^{2}\eta\geq 3C_{1}\eta
, which satisfies the fourth condition of (
121
).

•

If
\eta\leq\frac{1}{60L_{u}\kappa^{2}}
, then the condition
L_{u}\hat{\eta}\leq\frac{1}{4}
in (
121
) holds.
This condition also guarantees
1-3L_{u}^{2}\hat{\eta}^{2}\geq 0
.

•

If
\eta\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{10\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)}}
, then the last condition
5\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)\eta^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
of (
121
) holds.

•

If
\eta\leq\frac{2\sqrt{L_{u}}}{\kappa\sqrt{15(4L_{u}^{2}+\mu_{\psi}^{2})}}
, then the condition
(L_{u}+15L_{u}^{2}\eta)\hat{\eta}\leq 1
of (
121
) holds.

•

Finally, if
\eta\leq\frac{\sqrt{L_{u}}}{15\kappa\sqrt{2L_{u}^{3}\hat{\Lambda}_{0}(3\Theta_{u}+2)+\mu_{\psi}^{2}}}
, then the second condition
\hat{\Lambda}_{0}L_{u}^{2}(3\Theta_{u}+2)\hat{\eta}^{2}+30L_{u}^{2}\eta\hat{\eta}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}
of (
121
) also holds.

Overall, we can conclude that if we choose
\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}]
as in Theorem
9
, where

\begin{array}[]{lll}\bar{\eta}:=\min\Big\{\frac{1}{60\kappa^{2}L_{u}},\ \frac{1}{\sqrt{10\Lambda_{0}L_{w}^{2}(3\Theta_{w}+1)}},\ \frac{2\sqrt{L_{u}}}{\kappa\sqrt{15(4L_{u}^{2}+\mu_{\psi}^{2})}},\ \frac{\sqrt{L_{u}}}{15\kappa\sqrt{2L_{u}^{3}\hat{\Lambda}_{0}(3\Theta_{u}+2)+\mu_{\psi}^{2}}},\ \frac{1}{4L_{\Phi_{0}}+L_{w}+L_{f}}\Big\},\end{array}

then all the conditions in (
121
) are satisfied.
In addition, since
\mu_{H}\leq L_{u}
, we have
L_{u}+\mu_{H}\leq 2L_{u}
.
Thus the condition
\eta\leq\frac{1}{60\kappa^{2}L_{u}}
implies
\hat{\eta}\leq\frac{2}{L_{u}+\mu_{H}}
due to
\hat{\eta}=15\kappa^{2}\eta
.

Finally, to achieve
\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|{\mathcal{G}}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}
, we impose

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\frac{24[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\Psi_{0}^{\star}]+8[\Psi_{0}(\widetilde{w}_{0})-\mathcal{L}(\widetilde{w}_{0},\widetilde{u}_{0})]}{\eta(T+1)}+8\big(C_{w}+15^{3}\kappa^{6}C_{u}\big)\eta^{2}\leq\epsilon^{2}.\end{array}

If we choose
\eta:=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)\in(0,\bar{\eta}]
sufficiently small, and
T:=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
, then the last condition holds.

At each epoch
t
, Algorithm
2
requires
n
evaluations of both
\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
and
\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
.
Therefore, the total evaluation of
\nabla_{w}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
and
\nabla_{u}{\mathcal{H}}_{i}
is
\mathcal{T}_{e}:=nT=\mathcal{O}\big(n\epsilon^{-3}\big)
.
Similarly, since each epoch
t
, Algorithm
2
requires one evaluation of
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
, and one evaluation of
\mathrm{prox}_{\hat{\eta}_{t}h}
, the total number of both
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
and
\mathrm{prox}_{\eta_{t}f}
evaluations is
T=\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-3})
.
∎

Appendix D
Details and Additional Results of Numerical Experiments

This section provides the details of our experiments in Section
5
and also adds more experiments to illustrate our algorithms and compares them with two other methods.
All the algorithms we experiment in this paper are implemented in Python and are run on a MacBook Pro. 2.8GHz Quad-Core Intel Core I7, 16Gb Memory.

D.1
Details of Numerical Experiments in Section
5

We have abbreviated Algorithm
1
by
SGM
in Figure
1
.
Since we have two options to construct estimator
F_{i}^{(t)}
for
F(\widetilde{w}_{t-1})
, we name
SGM-Option 1
for Algorithm
1
using (
21
), and
SGM-Option 2
for Algorithm
1
using (
22
).

Implementation details and competitors.

Since
\phi_{0}(v)=\max_{\|u\|_{1}\leq 1}\{\langle v,u\rangle\}
in our model (
31
) is nonsmooth, we have implemented two other algorithms,
SGD
in
[
39
]
– a variant of the stochastic gradient method for compositional minimization, and
Prox-Linear
in
[
42
]
– a type of the Gauss-Newton method with variance-reduction using large mini-batches for compositional minimization.
Since
SGD
only works for smooth
\phi_{0}
, we have smoothed it as in our method, and utilized the estimator and algorithm from
[
39
]
, but also updated the smoothness parameter as in our method.
Here, we only compare the performance of all algorithms in terms of epochs (i.e. the number of data passes) and ignore their computational time since
Prox-Linear
becomes slower if
p
is getting large.
This is due to its expensive subproblem of evaluating the prox-linear operator.

To compare with
SGD
and
Prox-Linear
, we only use Algorithm
1
since both
SGD
and
Prox-Linear
are designed to solve compositional minimization problems of the form (
CO
).
However,
Prox-Linear
requires to solve a nonsmooth convex subproblem to evaluate the prox-linear operator.
Therefore, we have implemented a first-order primal-dual scheme in
[
6
]
to evaluate this operator, which we believe that it is an efficient method.

Parameter selection.

To boost the performance of all algorithms, we implement mini-batch variants of these methods instead of a single sample variant.
Our batch size
b
is computed by
b:=\lfloor{\frac{n}{k_{b}}}\rfloor
, where
n
is the number of data points and
k_{b}
is the number of blocks.
In our experiments, we have also varied the number of blocks
k_{b}
to observe the performance of these algorithms.
Since we want to obtain good performance, instead of using their theoretical learning rates, we have carefully tuned the learning rate
\eta
of all algorithms in a given set of candidates
\{100,50,10,5,1,0.5,0.1,0.05,0.01,0.001,0.0001\}
.
We find
\eta=5
(i.e.
\eta_{t}=10^{-4}
) for
w8a
and
\eta=100
(i.e.
\eta_{t}=5\times 10^{-5}
) for
rcv1
which work well for our method.
We also update the smoothness parameter
\gamma
as
\gamma:=\frac{1}{2(t+1)^{1/3}}
w.r.t. to the epoch counter
t
instead of fixing it at a small value.
For
w8a
, we find
\eta=0.05
as a good learning rate for both
SGD
and
Prox-Linear
.
For
rcv1
, we get
\eta=0.5
for both algorithms.
All experiments are run up to 200 epochs.

The convergence of gradient mapping norm.

Figure
1
only reveals the objective values of (
31
) against the number of epochs.
Figure
2
below shows the absolute norm of the gradient mapping
\|\mathcal{G}_{\eta}(\widetilde{w}_{t})\|
for this experiment.

Figure 2:

The performance of 4 algorithms for solving (
31
) in terms of gradient mapping norm.

It seems that both options,
SGM-Option 1
and
SGM-Option 2
are almost identical for this test.
For
w8a
, our methods look like having comparable performance with both
SGD
and
Prox-Linear
, just slightly better.
For
rcv1
, our methods reach a better approximate solution earlier than
SGD
, but after more than 200 epochs,
SGD
tends to approach a similar accuracy level.

Prox-Linear
has a significantly worse performance than ours and
SGD
in this particular experiment.

D.2
Additional Experiments

We provide additional experiments to test our algorithms and compare them with
SGD
and
Prox-Linear
as in Section
5
.

The effect of mini-batch size.

Our first test is to verify if the mini-batch size
b
actually affects the performance of these algorithms.
We use the same datasets and the same parameters as in Section
5
, but reduce
b
by increasing
k_{b}
from
32
to
64
blocks.
Figure
3
reveals the performance of 4 algorithms on two datasets with
k_{b}=64
:
w8a
corresponding to
b=777
and
rcv1
corresponding to
b=316
.

Figure 3:

The performance of 4 algorithms on two different datasets with
k_{b}=64
.

With this choice of mini-batches, our algorithms still have a similar performance as
SGD
, while
Prox-Linear
does not really improve its performance, and slightly gets worse.
Note that
Prox-Linear
requires a large mini-batch to achieve a variance reduce, and decreasing this mini-batch size indeed affects its performance.

Different learning rates.

Now, let us test our algorithms using different learning rates, we only focus on
Option 2
as both options show similar results in our tests.
For
w8a
, we choose
4
different learning rates
\eta=0.5,2.5,5.0
, and
7.5
, while maintaining
k_{b}=64
.
For
rcv1
, we also choose
4
different learning rates
\eta=25,50,100
, and
125
.
The results of this experiment are plotted in Figure
4
for both
w8a
and
rcv1
datasets.

Figure 4:

The performance of Algorithm
1
with 4 different learning rates
\eta
and
k_{b}=64
on 2 datasets.

As we can see from Figure
4
that

•

For
w8a
, our method starts diverging when
\eta=7.5
, while still works well for smaller learning rates.
For
\eta=0.25
, it indeed has a slow progress in early iterations as often seen in SGD.

•

For
rcv1
, we also observe similar behaviors as in
w8a
, but with larger learning rates than
\eta=125
.

Large dataset.

We have also run our algorithms and their competitors on a bigger dataset from LIBSVM:
url
with
n=2,396,130
and
p=3,231,951
.
Here, we use a learning rate
\eta=1
for our methods, which corresponds to
\eta_{t}=4.2\times 10^{-7}
.
For
SGD
, we use a learning rate
\eta=0.01
and for
Prox-Linear
, we use a learning rate
\eta=0.01
after tuning both methods.
We also set
k_{b}=64
for all algorithms.
The results of this experiment are reported in Figure
5
.

Figure 5:

The performance of 4 algorithms on a large dataset:
url
.

As we can see from Figure
5
, our methods have a comparable performance with their competitors.
All algorithms have similar behavior in terms of convergence.
